\subsection{Where the merge chose}

\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*]
\item \emph{Base text.} Source 01, which has the weakest hypotheses on most
shared statements (Pell with $D\ne0$, norm forms for any $\Q$-basis and
$c\in\Q^\times$, the only exhaustive quadratic dichotomy, the guard uniform in
$n$, the QE-free homogeneous criterion, the localization discussion).
\item \emph{Decomposable fibers.} Source 02's exact-fiber theorem is printed,
with source 01's rank-$n$ theorem as case (b) of
\cref{odg:thm:decomposable}.
\item \emph{Binary forms.} Source 05's complex form is printed; sources 01 and
02 proved the integer case.
\item \emph{Power equations.} Source 05's separated-power theorem replaces
source 01's gcd$>1$ corollary, which is kept as \cref{odg:rem:powers}.
\item \emph{Isotropic vectors.} Source 02's hypothesis $a\ne0$ replaces
source 01's $q(a)\ne0$ in \cref{odg:lem:nullchoices}.
\item \emph{Pairs without gcd.} Source 02's general theorem is printed; source
01's degree proof is kept as a second route (\cref{odg:rem:nogcd01}) because
it localizes to divisible workspaces.
\item \emph{Homogeneous systems.} Source 01's elementary proof and source 02's
Tarski proof are both kept, since only the latter handles sign conditions
and decidability.
\item \emph{Uniqueness of $\ct$.} Source 01's divisibility proof is printed;
source 05 derives it from the set-sized quotient theorem, which is quoted
and whose proof belongs to the sibling report.
\item \emph{Guards.} Both are printed: source 01's (five auxiliaries, three
squares) and source 05's (six auxiliaries, four squares).
\item \emph{Additions.} Two remarks carry the tag \mergetag:
\cref{odg:rem:separatedidentity} (a direct identity giving the divisibility in
the separated-power proof) and \cref{odg:rem:ringinduction} (existential
induction on the nonnegative cone fails in the pure ring language). Both have
complete proofs.
\item \emph{Definability base.} Source 06 is the base of
\cref{odg:def:sec:arithmetic,odg:def:sec:ct,odg:def:sec:reconstruction}: its
existential definitions of $\Pi$ and of the graph of $\ct$ supersede source
07's universal and prenex ones, which are kept as
\cref{odg:def:cor:universal,odg:def:rem:sigma2}, and its homomorphism theorem
covers all unital homomorphisms, where source 07's covers automorphisms.
\item \emph{The order-free system.} The two sources' systems are identical;
source 07's statement (both pairs, intermediate rings) is printed, with source
06 credited (\cref{odg:def:thm:constants}).
\item \emph{Real single polynomials.} The quartics of sources 01 and 05 stay
the printed versions. Source 07's quartic is source 05's up to the
substitution of \cref{odg:def:rem:quarticvariant} and is printed there once;
source 06's quintic, dominated by both, is a remark with the comparison
(\cref{odg:def:rem:quintic}).
\item \emph{Pell divisibility.} Source 07's permutation argument is printed
with source 06's finite-ring argument as a second route
(\cref{odg:def:lem:pelldiv}).
\item \emph{Norms.} Source 07's theorem (any base field, intermediate rings)
and source 13's (\'etale algebras, Gaussian variables) are combined in
\cref{odg:dec:thm:etale}; \cref{odg:thm:norm} of sources 01 and 02 is its
special case.
\item \emph{Fibers.} Source 13's real fiber theorem is
\cref{odg:thm:decomposable}(a), printed once with source 02 as base; its
Gaussian case, converse, kernel remark and rank-deficient example are added.
\item \emph{Transfer and obstructions.} Sources 06, 07 and 13 re-prove the
transfer theorem, printed once (\cref{odg:thm:transfer}); the collapse
obstructions of sources 06 and 07 generalize \cref{odg:prop:notdiophantine} and
are printed as \cref{odg:def:thm:collapse}. Source 07's recursive-saturation
statement is printed, with source 06's countable type as the simpler instance.
\item \emph{Fraction fields.} Source 06's common-denominator lemma is
\cref{odg:thm:fractions}, printed once; the Gaussian consequence is stated in
\cref{odg:def:sec:reconstruction}. Source 06's remark distinguishing $\ct$ from
$\st$ joins \cref{odg:rem:notvaluation}.
\item \emph{Four squares.} Source 06's appendix proof of Lagrange's theorem is
not reprinted; the theorem is cited (\cite{FourSquares} for Mathlib, source
07's \cite{Conrad}).
\item \emph{Further additions.} \Cref{odg:def:cor:ctquartic} (a quartic with six
witnesses for the graph of $\ct$, improving source 06's degree-ten polynomial),
\cref{odg:def:rem:numberfieldideal} (existential definitions over number-field
coefficients), the Gaussian example in \cref{odg:dec:rem:kernels}, the collapse
of the quintic over $\Og$ in \cref{odg:def:rem:quintic}, and the combined
\cref{odg:dec:thm:etale} carry the tag \mergetag{} and have complete proofs.
\item \emph{Fractions base.} Source 08 is the base of
\cref{odg:frac:sec:fractions}. Its multiplier theorem has the weaker
hypotheses: any domain with a retraction onto $\R$, any number of coordinates,
and any $t\in\Pi\setminus\{0\}$. Source 09 proves the pair version over $\BR$
and the rational functions of one monomial $t=\omega^\alpha$. So source 09's
multiplier theorem and one-scale classification are special cases of
\cref{odg:frac:thm:pullback,odg:frac:thm:main} and are not printed separately
(credited in \cref{odg:frac:sub:multiplier,odg:frac:sub:rational}). Source 09's
proofs use only $\BR$-unimodularity and full-class clearing, so they carry
over unchanged to source 08's setting.
\item \emph{The region $\Vreg$.} Source 09's formulation on the region $\Vreg$,
with $\Ureg=\spz^{-1}(\mathbb P^1(\Q))$, is kept as
\cref{odg:frac:thm:dichotomy}, proved from \cref{odg:frac:thm:pullback}.
\item \emph{Real constants and ordinary denominators.} Source 08's five
characterizations of rationality and source 09's example are printed once
(\cref{odg:frac:thm:realconstants}). Source 08's localization identity and
source 09's statements on units, subfields and B\'ezout pairs are combined in
\cref{odg:frac:prop:ordinary}.
\item \emph{Second routes.} The two B\'ezout lifts are kept: source 08's
\eqref{odg:frac:eq:lift} and source 09's adjustment of constants
(\cref{odg:frac:rem:lift}). The halving argument of both sources for the
absence of a gcd is kept next to the proofs of \cref{odg:thm:nogcd} and
\cref{odg:rem:nogcd01} (\cref{odg:frac:rem:halving}). The two non-rational
elements of one workspace (source 08's exponential series and source 09's
factorial-gap series) and the monomial examples of both sources are all printed
(\cref{odg:frac:ex:workspace,odg:frac:ex:monomial}).
\item \emph{Tables.} The two sources' example tables are merged into one
(\cref{odg:frac:ex:table}); the rows $m/n$, $r$ and $(t+r)/(t+1)$ occur in both.
\item \emph{Questions.} Source 08's question on set-generated denominator
ideals and source 09's on principal ideals outside $\Vreg$ are merged into
\cref{odg:frac:q:ideals}; the other three are printed as stated.
\item \emph{Credits added.} Source 08's conic example is
\cref{odg:prop:pythagoras} with source 01's witness and is not reprinted. Its
example without an lcm is, for monomial $t$, the sibling report's
\replabel{osq:tn:thm:syzygy}(iii), and its real-constant ideal is, for
irrational $r$, \replabel{osq:tn:thm:duals}. Source 09's localization
$\mathcal M^{-1}\Oz=\No$ is \replabel{osq:prop:fractions}(iii). Source 08's
Gaussian dichotomy contains manuscript 14's direction criterion, which is
printed once, crediting both (\cref{odg:frac:cor:gaussiandirections}).
\item \emph{Further additions.} \Cref{odg:frac:rem:halving} (the conclusion of
\cref{odg:thm:nogcd} in every $\Oz_\Gamma$), \cref{odg:frac:cor:homogeneous} (the
formulation of source 08's curve criterion for homogeneous systems) and
\cref{odg:frac:rem:elementary} (comparison with the elementary group of the
omnific groups report) carry the tag \mergetag{} and have complete proofs.
\item \emph{Curve base.} Source C14 is the base of
\cref{odg:cr:sec:principle,odg:cr:sec:curves,odg:cr:sec:groups}: on the shared
theorems it has the weakest hypotheses (any field of characteristic zero, any
exponent group, the curve classification over the field itself, the arithmetic
dichotomy with the generic fiber over $\Q$, unimodular coordinates over
$\mathcal R_{\mathfrak o}(\Bbbk,\Gamma)$). Source C11 has the most additional material
but states its classification over an algebraically closed field or $\R$; source C12
assumes an algebraically closed field throughout; source C13 assumes a divisible
exponent group and the fields $\R$ and $\C$; source C10 assumes an algebraically
closed field for curves.
\item \emph{Hidden hypotheses.} Proofs taken from sources with stronger hypotheses
were re-read before being attached to weaker ones. Source C12's logarithmic proof uses
closed points with residue field $\Bbbk$, so it is printed over an algebraically closed
field (\cref{odg:cr:thm:logroute}), and the general case descends as in source C14.
Source C13's tensor theorem, general Weierstrass corollary, curve corollary and group
kernel use only a functional $\Gamma_\Q\to\Bbbk$ and characteristic zero, and are
printed for every $\Bbbk$ and $\Gamma$
(\cref{odg:cr:thm:annihilation,odg:cr:cor:generalweierstrass,odg:cr:cor:allcurves,odg:cr:thm:groupkernel});
source C11's arithmetic fibers extend to every $\Bbbk$ by the classification
(\cref{odg:cr:thm:arith}). These extensions use the sources' own proofs.
\item \emph{Squarefree equations.} The strict-ideal proof of sources C12 and C13 is
printed (\cref{odg:cr:thm:squarefree}); the unit argument at the corner $(2,2)$ of
sources C10, C11 and C14 is kept as a second route (\cref{odg:cr:rem:unitroute}).
The support inclusion $\partial(\crA)\subseteq\crP$ is credited to
\replabel{osq:prop:classder} and \replabel{opa:par:prop:Euler}, which none of the five
cites.
\item \emph{Two rings.} Source C13's version for global tensors and symmetric
differentials is printed (\cref{odg:cr:thm:annihilation,odg:cr:thm:symmetric}); the
one-form versions of the other four are its case $r=1$. Source C14's bijection form
and source C11's equalizer lemma for families of maps are used
(\cref{odg:cr:thm:proper,odg:cr:lem:separated}).
\item \emph{Curves.} Source C14's proof of the classification is printed; source C12's
logarithmic proof is a second route; source C11's one-form remark and source C12's
projective-curve proof are combined in \cref{odg:cr:rem:oneform}; the real descent of
sources C10, C11 and C13 is the case $\Bbbk=\R$ of \cref{odg:cr:lem:formA1}.
\item \emph{Arithmetic.} Source C11's fibers over any coefficient ring are printed
(\cref{odg:cr:thm:arith}), with the fiber bijections of sources C10 and C13 and the
generic-fiber dichotomy of source C14 as \cref{odg:cr:cor:omnific}. Source C14's
integer arcs and congruence criterion, source C13's polynomial witnesses and source
C12's descent and coordinate ideals are added. The coordinate clearing reproved by
sources C10 and C12 is \cref{odg:cor:projectiveclear} and is not reprinted.
\item \emph{Groups.} Source C14's torus proof is printed, with the faithful-flatness
descent of source C10 and the complex descent of source C13 recorded; source C11's
quasi-finite theorem for any rigid target is printed, with the finite and semiabelian
cases of the others.
\item \emph{Further additions.} The closed-centre step in \cref{odg:cr:thm:logroute},
the comparison with the omnific groups report (\cref{odg:cr:rem:ogl}) and the
observation that source C13's characteristic-$p$ curve is an affine line (after
\cref{odg:cr:prop:charp}) carry the tag \mergetag{} and have complete proofs.
\item \emph{Logarithmic addition.} Source C15 arrived after sources C10--C14 had been
integrated. Its new results are appended as \cref{odg:log:sub:log}, at the end of
\cref{odg:cr:sec:groups}, so that no existing number moves; its other results are not
reprinted, and C15 is added to the credits of the statements it proves.
\item \emph{Routes of source C15.} Its squarefree proof is the strict route of sources
C12 and C13, with the element in both $\crP$ and $\crO$, and is not reprinted. Its
curve classification by one logarithmic form is described after
\cref{odg:log:cor:criterion}; it needs no closed-centre step. Its torus descent along a
finite splitting field is recorded after \cref{odg:cr:lem:torus}, and its unimodular
proof, through the principal ideal domains $\Z$ and $\Z[i]$, is that of
\cref{odg:cr:thm:unimodular}. Its descent theorem, for every subring of every field of
characteristic zero, is printed as \cref{odg:log:thm:descent}; \cref{odg:cr:thm:descent}
of source C12 is its case over $\Z$.
\item \emph{Hidden hypotheses in source C15.} Its curve classification is stated over an
algebraically closed field and its descent corollary over any field of characteristic
zero; both are covered by the statements above over every field. Its abstract
interface asks for a valuation ring $V\subseteq K$; the valuative criterion needs $V$ to
be a valuation ring of $K$, and \cref{odg:log:rem:interface} says so. No result is added
by the merge in \cref{odg:log:sub:log}.
\item \emph{Singular addition.} Source C16 arrived after source C15. It answers a question
of this part and shares its spine, so its new results are appended as
\cref{odg:sg:sub:singular}, at the end of \cref{odg:cr:sec:curves}, directly after the
subsection that states the problem; appending at the end of a section moves no existing
number. Its rings, Euler derivations, non-normality example, descent of forms of the affine
line and smooth case are not reprinted, and C16 is added to the credits of
\cref{odg:cr:lem:rings,odg:cr:lem:euler,odg:cr:lem:separation,odg:cr:lem:formA1,odg:cr:thm:affine}
and of the witness in \cref{odg:cr:sub:singular}.
\item \emph{Routes of source C16.} Its proof of the smooth case is recorded as a third
route (\cref{odg:sg:rem:thirdroute}). For the pinched elliptic curve both of its proofs are
printed, by the general theorem and by the explicit seventh-order certificate
(\cref{odg:sg:thm:example}); its B\'ezout polynomials are those of \eqref{odg:cr:eq:cubic}.
\item \emph{Stated generality of source C16.} Its results are printed at the generality it
states: the criterion over every field of characteristic zero, the one-place proposition
over an algebraically closed field of any characteristic, and the dimension-one theorem and
superelliptic test over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero. Three items
carry the tag \mergetag{} and have complete proofs: the consistency of the one-place
obstruction with the positive-characteristic examples (after \cref{odg:sg:prop:oneplace}),
the third route (\cref{odg:sg:rem:thirdroute}) and the superelliptic test over an arbitrary
field of characteristic zero (after \cref{odg:sg:thm:super}).
\item \emph{Questions of source C16.} Its eight questions are printed as stated
(\cref{odg:sg:q:lifting,odg:sg:q:real,odg:sg:q:order,odg:sg:q:engine,odg:sg:q:charp,odg:sg:q:birational,odg:sg:q:images,odg:sg:q:lean});
the first is the third clause of \cref{odg:cr:q:singular}, and the third, fourth and fifth
refine \cref{odg:cr:q:coefficients,odg:cr:q:charp}. They are not merged into those questions,
so that each is printed as its source states it.
\item \emph{Discriminant addition.} Source C17 arrived after source C16. It answers
\cref{odg:q:roots} for monic polynomials with a nonzero constant discriminant and the first
clause of \cref{odg:cr:q:coefficients} in dimension zero, and it shares the Euler and
two-ring mechanism of the curve part, but its subject is equations with omnific
coefficients. So it is appended as \cref{odg:disc:sub:disc}, at the end of
\cref{odg:sec:coefficients}, after the questions' own pointer to it; appending at the end of
a section moves no existing number, whereas a new section before \cref{odg:sec:formal}
would renumber Sections 20 and 21. Its ring, Euler and algebraic-constant lemmas are not
reprinted, and C17 is added to the credits of
\cref{odg:cr:lem:rings,odg:cr:lem:euler,odg:cr:lem:separation,odg:cr:cor:algclosed,odg:prop:univariate}.
\item \emph{Routes of source C17.} Both of its proofs of the translation theorem are printed,
the root-velocity certificate and the route through the unit theorem
(\cref{odg:disc:sub:second}). Its appendix on characteristic polynomials after reduction is
printed as \cref{odg:disc:lem:reduction}, its quadratic normalization as
\cref{odg:disc:rem:quadratic}, and its sign check in the text before
\cref{odg:disc:lem:certificate}.
\item \emph{Stated generality of source C17.} Its results are printed at the generality it
states, for every field of characteristic zero and every set-sized ordered group. Five items
carry the tag \mergetag{} and have complete proofs: the omnific roots at a constant
discriminant (\cref{odg:disc:cor:roots}); the comparison with the quadratic criterion and the
lifting example (\cref{odg:disc:rem:comparison}); the \'etale norm theorem as a constant case
(\cref{odg:disc:rem:etalenorm}); the comparison with the sibling report's canonical
translation (\cref{odg:disc:rem:osq}); and the second route for symmetric omnific matrices
(\cref{odg:disc:rem:symmetric}), with two sentences identifying the generic image in
\cref{odg:disc:ex:nonetale} and the two-ring pattern in \cref{odg:disc:sub:second}.
\item \emph{Questions of source C17.} Its ten questions are printed as stated
(\cref{odg:disc:q:etale,odg:disc:q:arithmetic,odg:disc:q:picard,odg:disc:q:certificate,odg:disc:q:defect,odg:disc:q:charp,odg:disc:q:restricted,odg:disc:q:several,odg:disc:q:dynamics,odg:disc:q:lean});
the fifth refines \cref{odg:q:roots} and the sixth sits beside
\cref{odg:cr:q:charp,odg:sg:q:charp}. They are not merged into those questions.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Printed once.} The floor formula, retraction, congruences, finite
quotients and completions, transfer, Hilbert's tenth problem, the Smith
criterion, units, the Euclid example, non-atomicity, the ACC chain,
$\Pi^2=\Pi$ and non-generation, $\Frac(\Oz)=\No$, the Pythagorean family, the
Fermat scaling, $x^2=\omega^2+1$, the orthogonal group, the nilpotent
isometry, the torus remark, and the failure of four squares for $\omega-1$
appear in two or three of sources 01, 02 and 05 and are printed once, with all
sources named. From sources 06, 07 and 13: the units lemma, Pell rigidity
(with the Gaussian solution $(i,i)$), the Pell divisibility lemma, the
intersective polynomial and its proof, the order-free system and its
Gaussian witness for $1+i$, the transfer theorem and Hilbert's tenth problem,
the collapse obstruction, $\Frac(\Oz)=\No$, the zero Pell fiber, the cubic
norm, the negative-exponent Pell solutions and the omitted types are each
printed once, with every proving source named. Sources 08 and 09 both reprove
the rings and their units, the common monomial divisor, the non-generation of
$\Pi$ and $\Frac(\Oz)=\No$ (credited by both to Conway and to
L'Innocente--Mantova \cite[Proposition 2.4.5]{LM}); source 09 also reproves
the floor, ordered division and $\Pi^2=\Pi$. Both recover the pair $(t,rt)$
without a gcd and the rational-direction theorem as special cases and credit
them. These are printed once, in
\cref{odg:sec:basic,odg:sec:class,odg:sec:primitive}. Within
\cref{odg:frac:sec:fractions}, the unimodular-fraction lemma, the real
constants, ordinary denominators, the one-function criterion, the monomial
criterion and the Gaussian fraction field are printed once with both sources
named. All five of sources C10--C14 reprove the rings, the retraction, the units, the
degree rules, the Euler derivations and their detection property, which are printed
once (\cref{odg:sec:basic,odg:sec:derivations,odg:def:lem:units}, extended in
\cref{odg:cr:lem:rings,odg:cr:lem:euler,odg:cr:lem:separation}), and all five credit
them to this report. Within
\cref{odg:cr:sec:principle,odg:cr:sec:curves,odg:cr:sec:groups}, the following are
printed once with every proving source named: the squarefree theorem and the cubic
certificate (all five), the singular Weierstrass families (C10, C11, C12, C14), the
two-ring principle and proper rigidity (all five), the canonical-bundle lemma (all
five), the puncture lemma (C10, C11, C13, C14), the curve classification (all five,
at different generality), the arithmetic fibers (C10, C11, C13, C14), unimodular
coordinates (C10, C13, C14), abelian and semiabelian rigidity (all five), the
quasi-finite theorem (C10, C11, C12, C14), the characteristic-two point (C11 and C14,
the same example with exponents rescaled by three), the fixed-workspace non-normality
examples (C10, C12, C13, with \cref{odg:prop:notnormal}), the field points outside the
ring (C10, C12, C13, C14) and the examples with nonconstant coefficients (C10, C12, C13,
C14). Source C15 reproves the rings, units, Euler derivations and joint constants, the
squarefree theorem, the cubic certificate with the example $\Delta_0=23$, the singular
families, abelian, torus and semiabelian rigidity, the quasi-finite theorem and
inheritance, the curve classification with $\mathbb P^1$, symmetric differentials and ample
cotangent bundles, unimodular coordinates on curves of positive genus, the
characteristic-two point (C11's example with the coordinates interchanged), the
$\Gamma=\Z$ integrality example of C14, and the field and valuation-ring points; these are
printed once, with C15 named in their credits, and the workspace lemma it uses is
\cref{odg:lem:workspace}. Source C16 reproves the rings, the retraction and the units,
the transcendence of nonconstant elements, the Euler derivations and their detection
property, the fixed-workspace non-normality example (for any $0<e<H$ with $ne<H$), the
descent of forms of the affine line and, as its smooth case, the smooth classification;
these are printed once, with C16 named in their credits
(\cref{odg:cr:lem:rings,odg:cr:lem:euler,odg:cr:lem:separation,odg:cr:lem:formA1,odg:cr:thm:affine},
\cref{odg:cr:sub:singular}).
Source C17 reproves the rings, the retraction, the units and the pullback
$\mathfrak o+\crP$ (its Lemma 2.1), the Euler derivations, logarithmic boundedness and
joint constants (its Lemma 3.1), the algebraic constants of the Hahn field (its Lemma 6.3)
and the example $\sum_m\omega^{1/m}$; these are printed once, with C17 named in their
credits
(\cref{odg:cr:lem:rings,odg:cr:lem:euler,odg:cr:lem:separation,odg:cr:cor:algclosed,odg:prop:univariate}).

\subsection{Corrections to the sources}

\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*]
\item Source 02 states Pell rigidity for $D>0$ (in its abstract, its
introduction, its catalogue table and its Pell corollary); its proof uses only
$D\ne0$, as in sources 01 and 05. \Cref{odg:cor:pell} states $D\ne0$.
\item Source 01's abstract says that nonzero fibers of ``nondegenerate binary
forms'' are rigid. Its theorem requires, and proves, the weaker hypothesis of
at least two distinct projective linear factors, which in degree at least
three is weaker than squarefreeness ($X^2Y$ qualifies). The abstract of this
report states the theorem's hypothesis.
\item Source 02 cites Matiyasevich's translation as \emph{Soviet Math.\
Dokl.}\ 11 (1970), 354--358, and source 05 as 354--357. The range 354--357 is the
one given, with MathSciNet number MR 258744, in the bibliography of Bhatt and
Poonen's notes \emph{Diophantine sets}, and in the title of the review of the
paper in the \emph{Journal of Symbolic Logic}; it is used here.
\item Source 05's proof of the set-sized quotient theorem invokes global
choice to pick a cardinal; the Hartogs ordinal suffices
(\cref{odg:rem:hartogs}).
\item Source 01's isotropic-vector lemma assumes $q(a)\ne0$ where $a\ne0$
suffices (\cref{odg:lem:nullchoices}).
\item Source 05's verification script checks the separated-power identity only
for $a=1$, $b=-1$; the general identity of
\cref{odg:rem:separatedidentity} was checked separately (symbolically, for
$2\le m,n\le7$) when this report was assembled. The shipped script is
unchanged.
\item Source 01's questions on $Y^2=X^3+1$ and on unimodular Fermat triples,
and the Fermat case of source 02's question on unimodular homogeneous
solutions, are recorded as settled by source 05
(\cref{odg:sub:settled}); the remaining parts are re-scoped as open
questions.
\item Source 02's attribution of the nonzero omnific Fermat failure to
MathOverflow comments could not be verified; it is printed as source 02's
attribution, marked unverified (\cref{odg:ex:fermatscaling}).
\item Source 06 presents its quintic (degree five, seven witnesses) as its
principal real Diophantine formula. The guards of sources 01 and 05, which it
did not see, have degree four with five and six witnesses; the quintic is
printed as a remark with that comparison (\cref{odg:def:rem:quintic}), and
source 06's question on the least degree is recorded as answered in part
(\cref{odg:def:sub:settled}).
\item Source 06's single real polynomial for the graph of $\ct$ has degree ten
and eight witnesses; \cref{odg:def:cor:ctquartic} gives degree four and six
witnesses.
\item Source 07's universal definition of $\Pi$ and its $\exists^5\forall^2$
and $\forall^2\exists^5$ definitions of the graph of $\ct$ are superseded as
definitions by source 06's existential ones; they are kept as the negation of
the detector and as a second route (\cref{odg:def:cor:universal,odg:def:rem:sigma2}),
and source 07's closing question on positive-existential definitions is
recorded as answered (\cref{odg:def:sub:settled}).
\item Source 13 states Pell rigidity for positive nonsquare $d$; its own fiber
theorem covers every $D\ne0$, which \cref{odg:cor:pell} states.
\item Sources 06 and 07 credit the intersective polynomial to L\^e--Spencer
(and 07 also to Berend--Bilu). L\^e and Spencer attribute it to Borevich and
Shafarevich \cite[p.~3]{BorevichShafarevich}, which is added as the classical
source.
\item Source 06 cites Matiyasevich's translation with pages 354--358, as source
02 does; the range 354--357 is used here, as explained above.
\item The formula $\exists y\,(x^2=2y^2)$ fails over number-field coefficient
rings containing $\sqrt2$ in their fraction field; source 06's remark on other
radicands supplies the replacement used in \cref{odg:def:rem:numberfieldideal}.
\item Source 09 says ``for finite $x$'' for points $x\ne\infty$ of
$\mathbb P^1(\No)$; ``finite'' means bounded here, so such points are called
affine (\cref{odg:frac:sub:conventions}).
\item Source 09's local density proposition concludes ``unless $g=f$
exactly''; the clause is unnecessary, since $g=f$ gives degree $-\infty$
(\cref{odg:frac:prop:localdense}).
\item Source 09's introduction speaks of ``a unique positive least
denominator''; a least positive denominator is unique by definition, and the
statement is \cref{odg:frac:thm:dichotomy}(i).
\item Source 08 writes $A=c^{-1}(\Z)$ for its abstract pullback and
$\mathcal A$ for the localization at $\Pi$, and orders projective coordinates
with the denominator first, source 09 with the numerator first. One order is
fixed here and both symbols are renamed (\cref{odg:tab:renames}).
\item Source 09's lemma on the matrices $\mathsf F_{x,b}$ is called
``lem:transvection'' in its source, the name of a different lemma here
(\cref{odg:lem:transvection}); it is \cref{odg:frac:lem:focusmatrix}.
\item Source 08 does not name this report's Pythagorean witness
\eqref{odg:eq:pythbezout}, which was in the report at its pin, although it
disclaims novelty for the conic. The sibling report's coefficient-lattice
results and its localization $\mathcal M^{-1}\Oz=\No$ were written after the pin
of sources 08 and 09. These credits are added (merge choices above).
\item Source C12's logarithmic proof of the curve classification takes ``the
centre'' $Q$ of the valuation-ring point without showing that $Q$ is a closed point.
If it were the generic point, the function field would lie in the valuation ring and
the residue map would embed it into $\Bbbk$, which is impossible for transcendence
degree one. This step is added (\cref{odg:cr:thm:logroute}).
\item Source C13 assumes a divisible exponent group, with $\lambda:\Gamma\to\Q$, and,
for curves and groups, the fields $\R$ and $\C$. Its proofs need neither; its results
are printed for every ordered exponent group and every field of characteristic zero,
with $\lambda:\Gamma_\Q\to\Bbbk$ as in sources C10 and C14. Sources C10 and C12 state
their curve theorems over algebraically closed fields and source C11 over such fields
or $\R$; the report states them over every field of characteristic zero by source
C14's descent.
\item Sources C10, C11 and C14 treat the corner $m=d=2$ of the squarefree theorem by
units. The support inclusion $\partial(\crA)\subseteq\crP$ used by sources C12 and C13
removes it; it is already in \replabel{opa:par:prop:Euler} and
\replabel{osq:prop:classder}, which none of the five cites. The strict proof is
printed first and the credit is added.
\item Source C13 cites Stacks Project Tag 02AT for K\"ahler differentials; that tag is
Section 111.50 of the chapter of exercises. The citation is replaced by Tag 00RM
(Section 10.131), which sources C10 and C14 cite. Source C13's Tag 01KF for the
valuative criterion was not checked; Tag 0BX5 (Lemma 29.43.1), cited by sources C12
and C14, is cited instead. Sources C10 and C11 cite Tag 0BX4, the section containing
that lemma, and Riemann--Roch is cited as Section 53.5 (Tag 0B5B) or as its Lemma
53.5.2 (Tag 0BS6); these are consistent. Milne's \emph{Abelian Varieties}, Chapter IV,
6.7 is called a proposition by sources C10 and C11 and a corollary by source C14;
this was not checked.
\item The non-integral-closure examples of sources C10, C12 and C13 repeat, in fixed
workspaces, the witness $\sqrt{\omega^2+1}$ of \cref{odg:prop:notnormal} (sources
01, 02, 05), which was in this report at their pins; only the fixed-workspace forms are
new, and the credit is added (\cref{odg:cr:sub:singular}).
\item Source C13's characteristic-$p$ example is correct as stated, a counterexample
to squarefree rigidity; its curve is, however, an affine line in characteristic $p$,
which is recorded after \cref{odg:cr:prop:charp}.
\item Source C15 says that its example $t^{-1}(1+t^2)^{1/2}$ for $\Gamma=\Z$, integral over
its ring but not in it, shows that ``normality really is unavailable, even in this simple
workspace''. For $\Gamma=\Z$ the ring $\crA=\Bbbk[\omega]$ is a polynomial ring and
normal; the element shows only failure of integral closedness in the larger
field $\crH$. It is outside $\Frac\crA$ and cannot obstruct the lift of
a nonconstant curve point over this normal ring. The corrected distinction is printed
(\cref{odg:cr:sub:singular}); it is the example of source C14, which disclaims
non-normality. Source C15's proof audit words the point correctly.
\item Source C15's abstract says that ``consequently'' every semiabelian variety is rigid,
after the logarithmic annihilation theorem. Its proof goes through the abelian quotient
and the torus kernel, as in \cref{odg:cr:thm:semiabelian}, not through the logarithmic
criterion; no claim is made here that semiabelian rigidity follows from
\cref{odg:log:cor:criterion}.
\item Source C15's abstract interface asks for a valuation ring $V\subseteq K$ containing
$k$; the proof needs $V$ to be a valuation ring of $K$, so that the valuative criterion
applies (\cref{odg:log:rem:interface}).
\item Source C16 introduces the element $t^{-1}(1+t^2)^{1/2}$ of the Hahn field ``over a
sufficiently divisible group''. The element lies in $\Bbbk((t^{\Z}))$, and for every
$\Gamma\ne0$ and $0<\gamma\in\Gamma$ the element $\omega^\gamma(1+\omega^{-2\gamma})^{1/2}$ lies in
$\crH$ and not in $\crA$; no divisibility is needed, and the statement is printed in that
form (\cref{odg:sg:sub:boundaries}).
\end{enumerate}
No mathematical error was found in any main proof of the seven sources, or in
the printed results of sources 13 and 14. In sources C10--C14 the only gap found is
the closed-centre step of source C12, supplied above; there is no false theorem and
no contradiction among the five, with the collection, or with the other sources of this
report. In source C15 no gap was found in the proofs; its remark on normality and the
logical route stated in its abstract are corrected above, and it contradicts no other
source. In source C16 no gap was found in the proofs; its one remark with an
unnecessary hypothesis is noted above, its one-place proposition is consistent with the
positive-characteristic examples of \cref{odg:cr:sub:charp}, and it contradicts no other
source. In source C17 no gap was found in the proofs, and it contradicts no other source. The later proof review of
\cref{odg:sec:transfer,odg:sec:rigidity,odg:sec:derivations} (recorded in
\fn{RECONCILIATION.md}) corrects the coefficient-derivation hypothesis in the
merge's own \cref{odg:rem:separatedidentity}: the derivation must kill $a$
and $b$, and its use at level $c$ needs $\partial c=0$; the Euler derivations
used in the proof satisfy both. The complex extensions of one-variable and
separated-power rigidity (\cref{odg:prop:univariate,odg:thm:separated}) are
explained after their proofs. A further review of
\cref{odg:sec:quadratic,odg:sec:obstructions,odg:sec:guard} expands the
polarization argument and the transfer in \cref{odg:thm:bounded}, which now
covers quantified ordered-ring formulas explicitly; it adds that the quadratic
orbits preserve the coordinate ideal, and it makes the nonnegative induction
domain and the order-free semiring formula of \cref{odg:rem:ringinduction}
explicit. It did not cover \cref{odg:def:rem:quarticvariant}.

\subsection{Stale repository statements}

Sources 01, 02 and 05 inspected the repository through the GitHub connector,
without a checkout or build, at the commit \pin{} named above; sources 06 and
07 inspected it at
\texttt{71e9606} in the same way, and source 06 also read the opening of the
surcomplex automorphism report. Sources 08 and 09 inspected it at
\texttt{9a385d3}, also through the connector.
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*]
\item Source 01 says that the repository's trigonometry material already uses
the omnific integer part. At the pin this was true but incomplete: the
foundations report already defined $\Oz=\Pi\oplus\Z$
(\replabel{found:eq:omnific}). The collection now also contains this report and
the sibling report on set-sized quotients.
\item All three sources correctly found no report devoted to $\Oz$ and no Lean
declaration about it at the pin. Both statements are now historical:
this report exists, and \cref{odg:prop:ring} has a Lean implementation.
\item Sources 02 and 05 describe \fn{docs/NORMAL_FORM_BRIDGE.md} and the
workspace modules; these descriptions are still accurate at the placement
commit.
\item The status of Conway's refinement conjecture is recorded as of
September 22, 2026 (\cref{odg:app:repo}); the transcendence-over-bounded-support
report takes the same stance.
\item Sources 06 and 07 say that the explicit Diophantine definitions of $\Z$
in $\Oz$ they give were not found in the repository. At their pin
\texttt{71e9606} this was true; since the placement \texttt{be06fc8} the
collection contains the quartic guards of this report
(\cref{odg:thm:guard,odg:thm:standarddef}). Source 13's statement that the
exact fiber formulation was not found is stale in the same way
(\cref{odg:thm:decomposable}). None of the three saw this report or its
sibling.
\item Source 06's descriptions of the surcomplex automorphism report (phase
twists that move the real axis while fixing $\C$) and of the catalogue entry
on coefficient recovery by a monomial dilation, and source 07's description of
the formalization ledger as separating source claims from Lean declarations,
are accurate at the placement commit \texttt{cf350b1}. The absence of omnific
Lean code was true then; \cref{odg:sec:formal} records the implemented ring package.
\item Sources 08 and 09 inspected the repository through the GitHub connector
at \texttt{9a385d3}, without a checkout or build. Both cite the
rational-direction criterion as ``Theorem 11.5'' of this report; it was 11.5 at

inserted as \cref{odg:def:sec:arithmetic,odg:def:sec:ct,odg:def:sec:reconstruction}.
Likewise source 09's ``\S\S4,11'' for the absence of lowest terms are now

\item Source 09 describes this report as merged from manuscripts dated
September 22, 2026. That was true at its pin, when the report had three

\item Source 08's bibliography gives the sibling report the title \emph{The
Universal Set-Sized Quotient of the Omnific Integers}. That was its title at the

\item Sources C10--C14 inspected the repository through the GitHub connector at
