\section{Non-claims by source}\label{odg:app:nonclaims}

Every limitation, disclaimer and priority caveat stated by a source is kept.
Most also appear at their point of use in the text; this appendix is the
per-source record. The non-claims of sources C10--C15 about singular curves record
what those sources claim; the singular-curve criterion is now supplied by source C16
(\cref{odg:sg:sub:singular}), within its own non-claims below. Likewise their statements
that no rigidity is asserted for coefficients varying at a surreal scale record what they
claim; source C17 supplies such a theorem in dimension zero, within its own non-claims.

\paragraph{Common to sources 01, 02 and 05.} No independent peer review; no Lean
or other proof-assistant verification; the finite scripts check finite
identities and examples, not the theorems; the repository was not checked out
or built; the Conway refinement announcement of September 18, 2026 was not
audited, is not used, and is neither assumed nor claimed settled; no result
assumes refinement, GCD or UFD properties; no priority claim is made.

\paragraph{Source 01 (24 items).}
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=1pt]
\item Proving a statement is not a claim that nobody proved it before; no
exhaustive historical-priority claim; the quadratic classification and the
guard are named as its most distinctive constructions, found in the inspected
sources in no such form.
\item Familiar mechanisms (coefficient retractions, unit arguments, polynomial
parametrizations, nilpotent quadratic isometries) are not claimed as new; a
focused search does not establish priority.
\item The script is a supplement, not a proof; it does not implement the
surreal numbers.
\item Transfer does not include inequations or order conditions.
\item Transfer concerns existence, not cardinalities or complete solution
sets.
\item The Hilbert's-tenth-problem statement concerns ordinary integer
coefficients; omnific coefficients are a different question.
\item Thue levels: absence of additional omnific solutions only; no new proof
of finiteness and no algorithm for ordinary solutions.
\item The gcd$>1$ power argument does not classify coprime cases such as
$x^2-y^3=1$ (now covered by source 05).
\item The torus remark does not say that every affine variety is constant.
\item The quadratic classification concerns represented nonzero levels; it
does not decide representability or classify all infinite points.
\item The guard's five auxiliary variables are not claimed optimal.
\item The first-order definition of $\Pi$ is not an existential definition.
\item Polynomial lifting does not license evaluating infinite power series.
(Status (batch 32): entire series over a fixed workspace are treated in
\fn{docs/surcomplex/entire-functions-at-arbitrary-rank};
by \replabel{ent:as:thm:sampling} and \replabel{ent:as:thm:integer} those
preserving the omnific part are polynomials.)
\item Fermat scaling solutions are not primitive and resolve no primitive
Fermat problem.
\item The rational-direction theorem concerns real projective points only.
\item Initial equations are necessary, not sufficient.
\item The converse of the no-real-point-at-infinity theorem is false.
\item Common divisors, clearing, $\Frac(\Oz)=\No$ and non-generation of $\Pi$
are full-class and not asserted for fixed workspaces; clearing is
representability, not primitivity.
\item The three-square theorem, MRDP and the normal-form foundations are
imported, not reproved.
\item L'Innocente--Mantova's Theorem B is not used in any proof.
\item None of its arguments refutes the refinement property.
\item Proposed module names are proposals; no Lean files; not checked against
a built repository.
\item A missing repository search match is not proof of absence.
\item Its questions are not all claimed open in the literature.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Source 02 (22 items).}
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=1pt]
\item The definition, integer-part property and pathological factorization
belong to established theory; the retraction and many consequences should not
be advertised as new.
\item The literature search does not establish priority for its principal
formulations (exact decomposable fibers, uniform quadratic lifting);
independent rediscovery is likely; absence of an identical formulation is no
evidence of priority.
\item AI-assisted; no peer review; no complete Lean verification; no Lean
build.
\item The checks do not prove transfinite-support results, the Diophantine
claims, or the imported quantifier-elimination and undecidability theorems.
\item Transfer says nothing about how many points lie above an integer point.
\item The common-multiple theorem is an existence theorem, not an algorithm;
no nonzero element is divisible by every nonzero omnific integer.
\item $\Pi/\Pi^2=0$ does not mean the infinite part is small.
\item The L'Innocente--Mantova prime is not reproved.
\item No effective enumeration of surreals is assumed; arbitrary class
coefficients have no implicit finite encoding.
\item Nonvanishing and order do not transfer; the retraction is not an
elementary embedding; residual solvability need not lift for non-integer
coefficients.
\item The failure of four squares does not contradict open induction.
\item Residual and initial tests are not a complete lifting criterion.
\item The homogeneous theorem does not decide affine omnific equations.
\item The Fermat example is not a refutation of Fermat's Last Theorem and not
a novelty claim.
\item Clearing and homogeneous solvability do not answer the
primitive-representative question.
\item Polynomial maps are not assumed to exhaust all constructions.
\item Binary and norm rigidity give no effective bounds for ordinary points.
\item Not every omnific linear group is finite.
\item The quadratic classification concerns the appearance of infinite points,
conditional on ordinary solvability; not every point lies on the orbit;
degenerate forms and the zero level are excluded from its statement.
\item Do not infer $\Pi_G^2=\Pi_G$ for fixed workspaces; restricted
coefficient fields need the vectors $e,u$ or a scalar extension.
\item Its questions: no claim that other fibers are rigid or come from curves;
no general root-membership algorithm (one would contradict H10).
\item Formalization stages are proposals; formalizing the retraction does not
formalize Tarski or MRDP; the repository review was targeted and the normal
form theorems are used as mathematical inputs, not as an audit of the
implementation.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Source 05 (26 items).}
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=1pt]
\item No general solution of omnific Diophantine equations and no effective
presentation of every surreal.
\item No new proof of the theorems of Lagrange, Matiyasevich, Shepherdson or
Wiles.
\item No historical-priority claim for the derived results.
\item Not peer reviewed; not formally verified; finite checks are not
substitutes for proofs and do not check arbitrary supports or the
class-theoretic pigeonhole argument.
\item The normal-form bridge implementation is not independently certified;
the Lean implementation was not built or audited.
\item The substantial factorization theory of L'Innocente--Mantova is a
separate achievement, not replaced by elementary arguments.
\item Transfer does not make fibers singletons.
\item The computational statement does not cover omnific coefficients.
\item Projective representability does not give unimodular representatives.
\item The Euler derivations are local and not canonical, and are not the
Berarducci--Mantova derivation.
\item Full-class results are not asserted for a preassigned workspace.
\item The set-sized quotient theorem does not apply to class targets such as
$\Oz$, and it does not say that endomorphisms are the identity.
\item The size conclusion for $\Oz/(f)$ concerns the class ring, not a
set-sized quotient.
\item There is no Pell solution at index $\omega$.
\item No Thue finiteness proof is contained in the reduction.
\item The conic statement covers central conics, not every quadratic
equation.
\item Separated-power rigidity gives no bound or complete list of integer
solutions; the script certifies no completeness.
\item $y^2=x^3+ax+b$ with $a\ne0$ is outside the method, a genuine boundary.
\item Only fixed ordinary exponents are treated, not variable omnific
exponents.
\item The Fermat theorem says nothing about triples that merely lack a common
nonunit divisor, and assumes no gcd equivalence.
\item The first-order definition of $\ct$ is not existential.
\item The four-square theorem is used only on ordinary nonnegative integers.
\item Transcendence of infinite omnific integers does not imply algebraic
independence of tuples.
\item Finite-support specialization does not turn infinite-support solutions
into arcs; finite-support search is not a complete decision procedure; matching
constant terms is not a sufficient solvability criterion.
\item Its questions are not an exhaustive list of established open problems.
\item Its formalization route is a proposal; declarations were not invented;
the size-sensitive layer must not be generalized to fixed Hahn groups.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Common to sources 06 and 07.} Unrefereed research drafts with
written proofs; no Lean files and no Lean-verified result; the repository was
not built; the finite checks are exact but do not certify quantified
statements over Hahn supports or the class interpretation; the literature and
repository comparisons were focused, not exhaustive, and a missing search hit
is not evidence of absence; no named classical factorization conjecture, and
no named longstanding conjecture, is claimed to be resolved; historical
priority is not asserted. Their pin \texttt{71e9606} predates both omnific
reports.

\paragraph{Source 06 (25 items).}
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=1pt]
\item First-order definability of $\Z$ in $\Oz$ and the nonsaturation of $\Oz$
are not new (\emph{nombre}, 2018); the ideal equation belongs to the same
obstruction and is not presented as an unrelated discovery.
\item Undecidability of integer-coefficient solvability follows from the
retraction and is not a new consequence; the logical limitations of $\Oz$
were discussed by Je\v r\'abek in 2011.
\item The intersective polynomial and its local-root property are classical;
Pell methods in definability have a long history \cite{Davis,Denef}; phase twists
are an existing repository construction, reproved with attribution.
\item No optimality assertion for degree five, seven witnesses or the
coefficient sizes of the quintic, nor for the Gaussian system.
\item The Gaussian predicate is a system; no single-polynomial Gaussian claim
is made, since a sum of squares does not preserve zero sets over a ring
containing $i$.
\item The certificate $vs=\Lambda(t)$ is not a global definition of
nonzeroness.
\item The multiplier and coefficient-field formulas are first order, not
positive existential; the finite valuation ring is defined in the interpreted
fraction field, not as a subset of $\Oz$.
\item The value group is recovered as an interpreted quotient, not a monomial
cross-section; no definability of the $\omega$-map, the simplicity order,
birthdays or the exponential is claimed.
\item Pointwise fixation of $\R$ is proved for automorphisms only, not for
nonsurjective embeddings.
\item The homomorphism theorem does not say that homomorphisms kill the ideal;
preservation, annihilation and pointwise fixation are different assertions.
\item $\Gamma\ne0$ is necessary for coefficient reconstruction, and the
fraction field of a fixed Hahn ring need not be the whole Hahn field
($\Gamma=\Z$ gives $\Bbbk(\omega)$).
\item Full-field reconstruction uses surreal bounds for sets of exponents and
the real closedness of $\No$; no common denominator for a proper class is
asserted; pointwise fixation uses the density of $\Q$ in $\R$, and a general
ordered coefficient field can have nontrivial automorphisms.
\item The c.e.\ classification concerns standard-supported sets and integer
coefficients; arbitrary surreal parameters are not finite inputs to an
algorithm.
\item The quantifier-free obstruction does not prove a lower bound on degree.
\item A formula defining the standard integers in this ring need not select a
standard copy in an elementary extension.
\item No convergence, analytic continuation, canonical complex logarithm or
infinite-height trigonometry is used; the only exponential is the ordinary
complex exponential of a real multiple of $i$.
\item Class statements are formula by formula: no uniform truth predicate, no
set of all surreals, no class of all class automorphisms.
\item Recovering $\C$ in the Gaussian case does not recover the real form or
conjugation; naming an involution gives the real structure explicitly rather
than reconstructing it.
\item The coefficient-recovery formulas give no Diophantine definition of $\Z$
in $\Q$ and no solution of Hilbert's tenth problem over $\Q$.
\item No named classical factorization conjecture is addressed.
\item No Lean files, no kernel-checked theorem, no repository rebuild; the
checks guard against mistakes and do not certify quantified statements.
\item Qualified novelty only; the comparison was focused, and a missing search
hit was not used as evidence of mathematical nonexistence.
\item Its formalization order is a proposal, not a report of completed work.
\item Its questions are not claimed to be recognized open problems.
\item Lagrange's four-square theorem is classical; its appendix proof is not a
new theorem (and is not reprinted here).
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Source 07 (19 items).}
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=1pt]
\item The normal-form machinery, the Pell method, the intersective polynomial,
the four-square theorem and the undecidability background are established and
credited; the literature check is targeted, not exhaustive.
\item No prime-element or factorization theorem is rediscovered, and the
historical conjecture list is not treated as a list of current open
problems; the ideal characterization proves no factorization, unique
factorization or normality property.
\item No single-equation claim in the Gaussian case; the real quartic is not
asserted over $\Og$, where it accepts every $x$.
\item Two universal variables, five existential witnesses and degree four are
upper bounds only.
\item The $\exists^5\forall^2$ and $\forall^2\exists^5$ bounds are not claimed
minimal, and no claim is made that the graph of $\ct$ has no existential
definition.
\item The detector needs a root of $\Lambda$ in the coefficient field; it fails
for $\Z+\omega\Q[\omega]$ and is not asserted for all intermediate rings.
\item The number-field theorem is not uniform in $K$ and does not apply as
stated to infinite algebraic extensions.
\item Its interpretation of $\Z[i]$ is not identified with a Diophantine
definition of $\Z$ inside $\Z[i]$; no such result is needed.
\item Undecidability and non-axiomatizability are consequences of established
results (Je\v r\'abek 2011, DPRM), not new undecidability theorems about $\Q$ or
number fields.
\item It does not show that $\Pi$ lacks a positive-existential definition (and
source 06 shows that it has one).
\item Its automorphism corollary concerns ring automorphisms of the
coefficient-restricted ring, not field automorphisms of $\No$ or $\No[i]$, and
does not say that an arbitrary field automorphism preserves $\Oz$ or $\Og$.
\item Norm rigidity needs a nonzero right side and separability; it does not
say that every variety has only constant points; coefficients outside $A$ are
read in the ambient ring.
\item $I=\bigcap nA$ must not be transferred to coefficient rings containing
$\Q$.
\item The results are not claimed for the full surreal or surcomplex fields
without the omnific restriction.
\item No efficient algorithm; witness existence, decision complexity and
computability of representations are different questions, and the witness
recipe assumes access to coefficient data.
\item Saturation and $\Th(A)$ are asserted for set-sized rings; class
statements are formula by formula, without a global satisfaction predicate.
\item The checks do not verify arbitrary Hahn supports, the all-moduli proof,
prime existence for number fields, the model-theoretic claims or the class
interpretation; the script must not be run with \texttt{python -O}.
\item Its formalization route is a proposal; no Lean module is supplied.
\item Its questions are not claimed to be previously catalogued open problems.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Source 13, for the results printed here (7 items).} Its other
non-claims are recorded in the sibling report.
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=1pt]
\item The fiber theorem classifies the infinite points above ordinary points
only; it gives no Thue or Baker finiteness and no enumeration or bound of the
ordinary solutions, including those of the cubic norm equation.
\item The zero level is excluded, and the exclusion is sharp.
\item The fiber is a disjoint union of constant-coefficient fibers, with no
assertion that the total solution class is a set.
\item The norm is evaluation of a finite polynomial, not a new norm on a class;
nonordinary surreal solutions with negative exponents exist, so the theorem is
about the nonnegative-support ring.
\item Equational transfer and the Hilbert's-tenth-problem statement transfer
the classical theorem and do not reprove it; inputs are finite integer
polynomials, no encoding of surreal parameters is postulated, and no
complexity claim is made for Gaussian or number-field problems.
\item The exact-fiber formulation is a proposed contribution, not a certified
priority claim; the search was bounded.
\item Unrefereed, not checked in Lean; the finite script does not verify
arbitrary Hahn sums; no claim is made about the status of Conway's refinement
conjecture.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Common to sources 08 and 09.} AI-assisted research drafts with
written proofs; not refereed; no Lean files and no Lean-verified result; the
repository was inspected through the GitHub connector at \texttt{9a385d3} and
not checked out or built; the SymPy checks are exact but finite and verify
identities and examples, not the theorems; historical priority is not
asserted, and each names its candidate contributions without certifying them.

\paragraph{Source 08 (25 items).}
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=1pt]
\item $\Frac(\Oz)=\No$ is classical (Conway; L'Innocente--Mantova, Proposition
2.4.5) and not claimed as new.
\item Hamkins's observations on the failure of lowest terms and on irrational
reals as omnific fractions are not new.
\item The repository's constant-term arithmetic, set-uniform clearing and
common divisors, the pair $(t,rt)$ without a gcd and the constant-direction
criterion are credited; the needed arguments are reproved, not imported.
\item The pullback construction and the use of coefficient retractions are
classical; L'Innocente--Mantova's Lemma 9.2.1 is a methodological precedent.
\item Integrality, nonflatness and non-finiteness of such extensions are
classical phenomena; the equational flatness criterion (Stacks 00HK) is
standard.
\item The novelty assessment is qualified: the search was targeted, not
exhaustive; no priority is claimed; a result may be a specialization of known
algebra.
\item No named longstanding conjecture is claimed to be solved.
\item The parameter $t$ must be nonzero and purely infinite; merely infinite
elements with a nonintegral constant are excluded.
\item The polynomial vector needs gcd one in one variable; a multivariate gcd of
one does not give a B\'ezout identity, and the several-parameter case is open.
\item Rational residues require a common real normalization to a primitive
integer vector before the principal generator is stated.
\item Primitivity and unimodularity are shown to coincide only for the
classified representations, not over all of $\Oz$.
\item Denominator ideals contain $0$; representations need a nonzero
multiplier.
\item The scale-defect dimension is a real dimension, not a module length.
\item Tensor products and $\Tor$ are taken only over set-sized rings.
\item $\res$ and $\st$ have different domains, and neither is a retraction of
$\No$ onto a smaller field.
\item There is no homomorphism $\No\to\R$ that sets $\omega=0$; $f(0)$ is a
substitution in a rational function.
\item Monic monomial denominators are more restrictive than arbitrary ones
($1/(\omega-1)$).
\item The class of surreals with primitive fractions is not a subring; this
concerns primitive representations, not the existence of fractions.
\item The classification covers tuples in $\R(t)$ and $\C(t)$ only; it does
not classify all $\Den(x)$.
\item There is no general flatness or normalization theorem for Hahn integer
parts.
\item The certificate procedure is effective only over exact coefficient
fields; arbitrary reals are not assumed computable.
\item The conic example illustrates the classification; it is not a new
construction of Pythagorean triples.
\item The checks do not verify the normal-form theorem, arbitrary supports,
class-size claims or universal quantifiers, and they are not a proof
assistant.
\item The repository audit read catalogues and guides through a connector whose
responses were sometimes truncated; theorem numbers are cited from the catalogue,
and an empty code search was not taken as proof of absence.
\item Its questions are not assertions that the problems are unstudied.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Source 09 (20 items).}
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=1pt]
\item $\Frac(\Oz)=\No$ is Conway's theorem and not new.
\item The repository's constant-term arithmetic and floor, small common
divisors, $\Pi^2=\Pi$ and non-generation, the fraction field, the pair without
a gcd and the rational-direction theorem are not relabelled as new.
\item It does not claim that every surreal lies in $\Vreg$, that
divisor-coprime pairs are unimodular, or that $\Oz$ is a GCD or B\'ezout
domain.
\item The five-fold equivalence is proved on $\Vreg$ only.
\item It does not claim that a fixed Hahn field has the full-class clearing
properties; in a workspace a formal cancellation does not give a unimodular
pair.
\item No theorem needs Conway's refinement conjecture or any announced proof
of it.
\item $\spz$ is not a field homomorphism, does not extend $\ct$, and is defined
only on $\Vreg$.
\item The dense fibers are not open and not separated; focusing fails for
proper classes.
\item The one-scale test at $T=0$ is a formal specialization, not a surreal
value and not a valuation residue.
\item The topology at infinity on $\R(T)$ is not the surreal order topology.
\item The certificate procedure needs exact coefficients and is not uniform
for reals given by oracles; the checks are not a surreal computer-algebra
system.
\item It gives no complex lowest-term classification (source 08 does).
\item ``Reduced'' in L'Innocente--Mantova is a support notion and is not used
for lowest terms.
\item The pullback and matrix-completion methods are ordinary algebra; Klawa is
cited for background only; no invention of pullbacks, projective coordinates or
unipotent matrices is claimed.
\item The candidate contributions are its multiplier and dichotomy theorems,
the one-scale classification, and the orbit and focusing theorems; no
priority is established.
\item The literature search was keyword-based; a failed match is not evidence
of absence.
\item The checks do not prove well-ordering of supports, proper-class
separation, non-generation of ideals, the theorems or novelty.
\item Its formalization guide is a proposed order, not code; writing
\texttt{Set} in a statement does not discharge a universe condition.
\item The repository was not cloned or built, and only selected sources were
reviewed, not every manuscript in its history.
\item Its questions are the article's own, not certified open problems.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Source 14, for the result printed here (2 items).} Its other
non-claims are recorded in the sibling report.
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=1pt]
\item The unimodular-direction criterion concerns coordinate representatives,
not all points of a projective scheme over a ring.
\item The example $[1:\sqrt2]$ is not new; it predates manuscript 14.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Common to sources C10--C14.} AI-assisted research drafts with written
proofs; not refereed or independently reviewed; no Lean files and no Lean-verified
result, and the repository's build and axiom audit are not a verification of these
results; the repository was inspected through the GitHub connector and not built; the
finite checks verify identities and finite-support examples, not arbitrary infinite
supports, properness, Riemann--Roch, global generation or the class transfer, and a
count of passing checks is not a formal proof; historical priority is not established,
the searches were targeted, and a missing search hit is not evidence of absence; the
question answered is a continuation question of this report, not a literature
conjecture, and no named long-standing conjecture, in particular none of Conway's
factorization or refinement questions, is claimed to be solved; nothing is claimed
about singular curves in general, about general affine varieties of higher dimension,
or about primitive, non-unimodular Fermat triples; no enumeration, finiteness
statement, height bound or algorithm for the ordinary integer points is given; no
rigidity over $\No$, $\No[i]$ or the valuation ring is claimed, and field points outside
the rings exist; coefficients must be constants; the Euler derivations are local, not
canonical and not the Berarducci--Mantova derivation; no spectrum of a proper-class
ring is formed; the standard inputs (Hahn arithmetic, K\"ahler differentials, the
valuative criterion, smooth completion, Riemann--Roch, invariant differentials) are
imported and credited, and the constant term, units, Euler derivations,
separated-power and unimodular Fermat rigidity and finite-support specialization of
this report are credited, not claimed.

\paragraph{Source C10 (14 items).}
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=1pt]
\item The finite-polynomial special case, that maps from an affine line to a curve of
positive genus are constant, is standard and not claimed as new.
\item The weaker verticality conclusion $f(x)=f(\ct x)$ is not a claim of rigidity for
positive-dimensional fibers.
\item No equivalence with uniruledness, rational connectedness or the existence of
polynomial curves is asserted in higher dimension.
\item The clearing lemma may fail when the clearing exponent is confined to a fixed
workspace.
\item $\Frac(\crA)=\crH$ is not assumed; normality and unique factorization are not
assumed; ambient roots are never truncated into the ring.
\item The two-ring argument does not assert a ring map from the ring to the valuation
ring; properness supplies a second point, not an inclusion of rings.
\item Failure of global generation identifies the possible surviving directions; it
does not prove that they integrate to nonconstant Hahn points.
\item The arithmetic criterion is not an enumeration algorithm, decides no arbitrary
Diophantine system and uses no finiteness theorem for integral points.
\item Replacing a singular curve by its normalization changes the point problem unless
lifting is justified; that issue is not hidden in the smooth completion.
\item The argument is not a positive-characteristic theorem.
\item No rigidity is asserted for equations whose coefficients vary at a surreal scale.
\item Its suggestions of other coefficient systems and of iterative higher derivations
are suggestions, not results.
\item Its questions are boundaries of its arguments, not open problems certified in the
literature.
\item A native code search that returned no match is not evidence of absence.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Source C11 (15 items).}
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=1pt]
\item No integer-part assertion is made for an arbitrary subring $\mathfrak o$ or an
arbitrary $\Gamma$.
\item $\mathcal A_S(\Gamma)$ is in general not $S\otimes_\Bbbk\crA$; the reduced-base
principle is not faithful flatness.
\item Van den Dries (1981) was compared through its bibliographic record and abstract
only; no theorem-by-theorem comparison is made, and its discretely ordered ring theorem
uses nothing from that paper.
\item The Artin--Schreier telescoping identity is not claimed as new.
\item No implication from the absence of rational curves to rigidity is proved.
\item The pointwise criterion is an obstruction, not a classification of varieties
whose global forms fail to generate somewhere.
\item The curve-subalgebra corollary needs a smooth algebra, does not say that every
finitely generated subalgebra is a polynomial ring, and does not cover higher
transcendence degree.
\item The surcomplex conclusion does not say that the curves have no nonconstant points
in $\No[i]$; such points exist.
\item The projective theorem does not settle the primitive, non-unimodular Fermat
question, and projective points over a ring are not arbitrary homogeneous tuples over
its fraction field.
\item Integer coefficients cannot be replaced by omnific coefficients without
qualification.
\item The canonical integer part need not contain a copy of every discretely ordered ring
inside $\No$.
\item Conway's and Gonshor's texts were not newly read; for L'Innocente--Mantova only
the abstract and version information were checked, and their factorization theorems are
not used.
\item Enlarging $\Gamma$ to its divisible hull is permitted but not needed, and no
cofinal set in the class of surreal exponents is used.
\item Its build audit concerns its own artifact, not a mathematical proof.
\item Its questions are proposed continuation questions, not assertions of open status
in the literature.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Source C12 (13 items).}
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=1pt]
\item The definition of $\Oz$, the Hahn construction, the retractions, the degree
estimates and the Euler derivations are not claimed as new.
\item Only the sufficient direction of the coordinate test is used; no claim is made that
every projective point over the ring has a global unimodular tuple.
\item The ideal of its elliptic example has an explicit common nonunit divisor and does
not answer the primitive question.
\item Reducible affine curves are not classified.
\item Its formalization route is a proposal; no formalization was done.
\item The descent theorem is a reduction, not an algorithm for integer points; the
squarefree theorem does not decide the remaining ordinary questions; no effective
enumeration or height bound is given.
\item The Euler contractions are not claimed to separate all elements of
$\Omega^1_{\crH/\Bbbk}$; they separate functions.
\item No characteristic-$p$ classification is given.
\item In higher dimension one logarithmic form does not suffice, and no assertion is made
that it does.
\item Group structure alone does not give rigidity; the result is not about every
algebraic group ($\Ga$, $\SL_2$).
\item Uniform normality of the ring across exponent groups is not assumed.
\item Nonduplication is not certified against every supplementary manuscript, historical
commit or paper; the 51-report collection of its pin was not audited exhaustively.
\item Its symbolic checks guard against transcription errors; they do not prove the
arbitrary-support lemmas, the compactification argument or the coordinate-ideal theorem.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Source C13 (13 items).}
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=1pt]
\item No conclusion is drawn from nefness, bigness or general type; ``ample'' means the
literal tautological-bundle condition, and conventions for semiample bundles vary.
\item The splitting of group points is not an algebraic splitting of the group, and not a
formal-completion approximation.
\item Its non-normality example is not itself a failed lift of a point of a constant
curve, and a normalization lift may not be declared to exist.
\item Embedding a singular curve in an abelian variety is a substantial extra hypothesis;
the singular-subscheme consequence does not classify singular curves.
\item The abelian theorem is not $A(\crH)=A(\Bbbk)$ and not a statement about torsion points.
\item The polynomial-witness principle is not asserted for singular curves or in higher
dimension, and gives no uniform algorithm for integer points.
\item No resolution of Conway's factorization or refinement questions, or of any named
conjecture, is claimed.
\item Its 12,874 assertions are not independently verified theorems; the checks do not
verify the valuative criterion, the sheaf-theoretic contraction, Riemann--Roch, group
structure, the class assembly, correctness of every proof or originality.
\item Division by $2$ takes place in $\crA$; $2$ is not claimed to be a unit of $\Oz$.
\item The Fermat case is a specialization of an earlier result, not a new Fermat
theorem, and nothing is concluded about primitive, non-unimodular tuples.
\item $\mathcal C(\Oz)=\mathcal C(\Z)$ is neither an algorithm nor a finiteness statement,
and no undecidability problem is solved.
\item No theorem is asserted to have a matching Lean declaration; its formalization order
is a proposal.
\item Its example equations come with no claimed complete list of integer points.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Source C14 (12 items).}
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=1pt]
\item The integral-closure remark for $\Gamma=\Z$ does not assert that $\Bbbk[\omega]$ is
not normal.
\item The congruence criterion is an algorithm only after a rational parametrization and
its inverse are supplied, not for arbitrary curves.
\item Varieties with no global one-forms are not classified; global generation is
sufficient, not claimed necessary.
\item The supplementary definability manuscripts were not reviewed line by line, and the
repository was not audited exhaustively.
\item The singular families are polynomial families; not every point of a singular curve
is claimed to have a parameter in the same ring.
\item The arithmetic dichotomy permits bad reduction but not a singular generic fiber, and
gives no algorithm for integer points on curves of positive genus.
\item The factorization results of Berarducci and of L'Innocente--Mantova, and Conway's
refinement problem, are neither claimed nor solved.
\item Van den Dries (1981) was consulted through its record and abstract only; no
exhaustive comparison with its consequences, or priority over them, is claimed.
\item For a Fermat curve, excluding particular ordinary points needs separate arithmetic
input.
\item A nonzero exponent group is needed only for the converse of the curve classification.
\item No assertion is made that field-valued surreal points are constant.
\item A finite check count is not a formal proof of the paper.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Source C15 (18 items).}
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=1pt]
\item Its geometric package is a candidate-original contribution; priority is not
certified, targeted searches found no published statement of the whole package, and
negative search results do not prove novelty; not every repository companion was read.
\item Van den Dries (1981) was inspected through its bibliographic record and abstract
only, and overlap beyond the abstract is not excluded; its statements concern the
support-defined Hahn ring and its omnific subring, not a new criterion for all discretely
ordered rings.
\item Not refereed; no Lean verification; the repository was not built in its
investigation.
\item It settles no named long-standing conjecture, only a stated limitation of this
report, and supersedes neither the theory of integer parts nor the classical
algebraic-geometric ingredients.
\item No computation, enumeration, finiteness statement or height bound for the ordinary
integral points; it does not replace Siegel's theorem or methods for integral points,
and its example equations come with no computation of their ordinary solutions.
\item No classification of singular curves.
\item It decides no arbitrary omnific Diophantine system and says nothing about
Hilbert's tenth problem.
\item Normal forms, Hahn multiplication, the constant-term retraction, the classical
geometry of curves and algebraic groups and the Euler derivations are not claimed as
new; the Fermat case is prior and not claimed.
\item No statement about general type: positivity of the canonical bundle alone need not
supply tangent-separating symmetric differentials; no equivalence between rigidity and
the absence of nonconstant maps from $\Aff^1$ is established, and none may be inferred
from the curve classification.
\item No spectrum of a proper-class ring is formed; $\Frac(\crA)=\crH$ is not assumed; no
morphism between $\Spec\crA$ and $\Spec\crO$ is asserted.
\item The theorems concern points over the support-restricted rings, not over $\No$ or
$\No[i]$, where elliptic curves have nonconstant points; field solvability and
integer-part solvability are different problems.
\item Affine integer points are not projective integer points, and clearing field
denominators by a monomial does not create unimodularity.
\item Quasi-finiteness is essential ($\Aff^1\times G\to G$).
\item $\crA$ is not assumed normal, and lifting a point to a normalization is not an
available unstated step.
\item It is not asserted that every nontrivial form of $\Aff^1$ or $\mathbb P^1$ over an
arbitrary field has a nonconstant point; faithful flatness of the coefficient extension
in the descent is neither asserted nor needed.
\item The finite checks (2,820 cases, most of them valuation-margin samples) do not verify
the infinite-support or scheme-theoretic assertions.
\item A conditional Lean theorem using its geometric hypotheses would not by itself verify
the instantiations; its formalization plan is a proposal.
\item Its proof audit is an author-side audit, not an independent referee report or a
machine verification, and supplies neither priority nor independent proof checking.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Source C16 (19 items).}
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=1pt]
\item Characteristic zero is required for the criterion. In positive characteristic only
the one-place obstruction is proved; the genus-zero conclusion is not asserted, and
Seidenberg's Hasse--Schmidt operators are relevant but not claimed to supply a
valuation-detecting operator.
\item Constant coefficients are required; nothing is claimed for coefficients that vary at a
surreal scale.
\item Geometric integrality, dimension one and finite type are required; the theorem on
affine schemes of dimension at most one is stated over an algebraically closed field only.
\item The criterion is an existence and classification statement: it does not claim that
every nonconstant point lifts to the normalization, nor that every point is a polynomial
family evaluated at a Hahn parameter.
\item The real singular arithmetic fibers are not classified; a real normalization preimage
is shown sufficient, not necessary, and the isolated real node is left open.
\item No description is given of the fibers or the image of $\ct$ on exceptional curves, or
of the support order types in them.
\item The derivative-order bound is not claimed optimal, and no algorithm is given for
normalizations, conductors or regular differentials of an arbitrary presentation.
\item Ordinary integer-point existence is not decided, and no integer points are enumerated,
for the pinched curve or otherwise.
\item Nothing is claimed about points over the Hahn field, $\No$ or $\No[i]$; it is not a
claim that all field-valued surreal points are constant.
\item No higher-dimensional statement is made, and the curve argument does not subsume the
existing higher-dimensional criteria.
\item Full surreal statements are made only after reduction to set-sized workspaces; no
spectrum of a proper-class ring is formed.
\item Not refereed and not formalized in Lean; its formalization order is a proposal, and no
stage of it is claimed done.
\item Historical priority is not asserted: the targeted comparison is not exhaustive, and
``complete criterion'' refers only to the stated class of curves and points, not to a
named long-standing problem.
\item Van den Dries (1981) was compared through its bibliographic record and abstract only;
no comparison with its proofs is claimed.
\item Seidenberg's identity, the smooth classification, normalization, Riemann--Roch,
Riemann--Hurwitz, Hahn normal forms, the retraction, the units, the Euler derivations and
the non-normality of the ring are credited, not claimed.
\item The 113,940 finite assertions are regression checks, not counts of verified
theorems; they do not verify infinite Hahn series, normalization of arbitrary curves,
Riemann--Roch, Riemann--Hurwitz, the universal conclusions or novelty.
\item A clean compilation and the checksum manifest are not substitutes for mathematical
review or formal verification.
\item The repository comparison was not exhaustive: the assembled article was not read in
full, and no report, historical source, unmerged manuscript or Lean module was reviewed line
by line.
\item Its questions are proposed continuations, not recognized open problems of the
literature.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Source C17 (22 items).}
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=1pt]
\item It does not show that every finite \'etale $\crA$-algebra is constant: the injection
$\mathsf C_{\mathsf B}\to\mathsf B/\crP\mathsf B$ is not shown surjective, and full descent is
left open.
\item It does not assume or produce a global primitive element; a generic primitive element
does not give a monogenic presentation over $\crA$.
\item The unit theorem is not extended to $\mathfrak o+\crP$; arithmetic finite \'etale
algebras may have richer unit groups, and reduction modulo $\crP$ need not be a product of
fields.
\item A nonzero constant discriminant is distinguished from a unit discriminant throughout;
the translation theorem does not assert \'etaleness when the discriminant is not a unit.
\item No factorization, prime-element, unique factorization or refinement result, and no
birthday result, is claimed; L'Innocente--Mantova's factorization theorems are neither
reproved nor claimed.
\item No large cardinal or other set-theoretic strength is used or claimed, and no
proper-class finite-projective formalism is introduced.
\item No preferred derivation of the surreal field is constructed; the Euler derivations act
on coefficients, are not $d/dX$ and are not the Berarducci--Mantova derivation.
\item The exponent $2s$ in the certificate is not claimed optimal.
\item The Galois statement depends on the chosen coefficient field; the rational Galois group
is not claimed to survive extension of constants to $\R$.
\item The critical-point theorem is a classification up to independent source and target
translations, not a conjugacy, and says nothing about iteration.
\item The main polynomial and matrix theorems are false without one-sided support,
characteristic zero or a nonzero discriminant, and the matrix theorem without normality;
finite free and reduced does not suffice for the unit theorem.
\item The characteristic-polynomial theorem alone controls eigenvalues, not entries or
eigenspaces.
\item The polynomial-parameter case $\Bbbk[Y]$ and its multivariable form are not claimed new
and not presented as a deep theorem about the affine line; they answer the cited Stack
Exchange question as special cases only.
\item The coefficient test is an algebraic criterion, not an algorithm for arbitrary
presentations of infinite Hahn series.
\item No physical prediction about infinitesimal observables follows from the matrix
theorem.
\item The 1,126 finite assertions check identities, normalizations and examples; they do not
verify arbitrary supports, all degrees, the existence of the certificate in every degree,
the formal \'etale argument, or priority.
\item A clean build and the build report are production checks, not mathematical review.
\item Not refereed and not formalized in Lean; the repository's Lean coverage does not cover
these statements, and the formalization order is a proposal.
\item No novelty or priority is certified: the comparison was focused, not exhaustive, and a
prior equivalent result may exist under other names.
\item The repository comparison read some material through excerpts or a truncated catalogue
response; a search without useful matches is not evidence of absence.
\item The Hahn and omnific background, the Stacks foundations and the repository's
support, Euler and two-ring antecedents are credited, not claimed; the elementary support
facts are reproved independently.
\item Its questions are questions left open by its article, not a certified catalogue of
previously unknown open problems.
\end{enumerate}

\section{Dependencies and verification}\label{odg:app:verification}\label{odg:app:dependencies}

\subsection{Imported inputs}

The foundation imported from surreal theory is the existence, uniqueness,
order and multiplication of set-supported Conway normal forms, the set-cut
property, and the real closedness of $\No$ \cite{Conway,Gonshor}. The other
imported theorems are: MRDP (\cref{odg:cor:H10}); Tarski's quantifier
elimination (\cref{odg:thm:bounded}, \cref{odg:thm:cone}(b)); the three-square
theorem (\cref{odg:lem:pellgap}); Lagrange's four-square theorem
(\cref{odg:thm:standarddef}, \cref{odg:cor:H10}); Shepherdson's theorem (open
induction); the Fermat theorem of Wiles and Taylor--Wiles
(\cref{odg:cor:flt} only, after the rigidity theorem has reduced to ordinary
integers); the algebraic closedness of $\C$ (to factor binary forms; the
algebraic closedness of a class-sized surcomplex field is not used); and, as
context only, the primes of \cite{LM} and \cite[Lemma 11.1]{EK}. The material
of sources 06 and 07 imports in addition MRDP (\cref{odg:def:thm:ce}),
Lagrange's theorem (\cref{odg:def:cor:naturals,odg:def:rem:quintic,odg:def:rem:quarticvariant}),
Dirichlet's theorem on primes in progressions and quadratic reciprocity
(\cref{odg:def:lem:tailored} only), the existence of algebraic closures and
the discriminant criterion for \'etale algebras (\cref{odg:dec:thm:etale}), and
the real closedness of $\No$ (\cref{odg:def:thm:realrecovery}); the local
arithmetic of $\Lambda$, Hensel lifting and the Chinese remainder theorem are
proved in the text. The material of sources 08 and 09 imports only the
normal-form foundations, the set-cut property, ordinary polynomial and integer
B\'ezout identities, the Euclidean property of $\Z[i]$, the equational
criterion of flatness \cite[Tag 00HK]{StacksFlat} (\cref{odg:frac:thm:nonflat})
and standard tensor exact sequences (\cref{odg:frac:prop:tor}), and it uses
\cref{odg:frac:rem:elementary}'s citation of the omnific groups report.
The material of sources C10--C14 imports the universal property of K\"ahler
differentials \cite[Tag 00RM]{StacksDiff}; the valuative criterion of properness for
arbitrary valuation rings \cite[Tag 0BX5]{StacksProper}; smooth projective completion
of curves, Riemann--Roch with duality, and the genus-zero classification
\cite{StacksCurves,StacksRR,StacksGenusZero}; invariant differentials on algebraic groups
\cite{StacksGroups,MilneAV}; the functorial description of projective space, the
projective bundle of a locally free sheaf and globally generated powers of ample bundles
\cite{StacksProjSpace,StacksProjBundle,StacksAmple} (\cref{odg:cr:lem:coordinatepoint,odg:cr:cor:semiample});
the Barsotti--Chevalley theorem and the structure of commutative algebraic groups
\cite{MilneAG} (\cref{odg:cr:thm:groupkernel} only); and the Nullstellensatz, the
square-zero description of tangent vectors, the smoothness of normal curves over a
perfect field and the genus of a smooth plane cubic. It also uses
\cref{odg:cr:rem:ogl}'s citations of the omnific groups report. The material of source
C15 uses in addition differentials with logarithmic poles along a simple
normal-crossings divisor (\cref{odg:log:lem:valuation}). The local
construction and coordinate-independence are spelled out before that lemma;
\cite[Tag 0FMU]{StacksLog} treats the one-smooth-divisor construction,
and \cite[Tag 0BI9]{StacksSNC} records the local normal-crossings condition.
Its other inputs are among those just listed.
The material of source C16 imports in addition the finiteness of normalization for curves
\cite{StacksNormal,StacksCurves}, Seidenberg's identity \cite{Seidenberg} (reproved in
\cref{odg:sg:lem:seidenberg}), the free generation of local differentials by a
uniformizer and the Riemann--Hurwitz formula \cite{StacksRH}, and Kummer theory over a
field containing the roots of unity (\cref{odg:sg:sub:super}).
The material of source C17 imports in addition the algebraic closedness of Hahn fields with
divisible exponent group and algebraically closed coefficients \cite[Fact 2.1.1]{LM}, the
fundamental theorem on symmetric polynomials, the definition and base change of \'etale
ring maps and the structure of finite \'etale algebras over a field
\cite{StacksEtale,StacksEtaleField}, formal \'etale lifting \cite{StacksFormal}, the
discriminant criterion for finite locally free algebras \cite{StacksDisc}, and local
characteristic polynomials of finite projective modules (\cref{odg:disc:lem:reduction}).
All other deductions are proved here from these and finite algebra.

The principal dependency chains are:
\begin{align*}
 &\text{normal forms}\ \Longrightarrow\ \ct\text{ is a retraction}
 \ \Longrightarrow\ \text{transfer and finite quotients};\\
 &\text{normal forms}\ \Longrightarrow\ \text{constant units in }\BC
 \ \Longrightarrow\ \text{decomposable, binary and norm rigidity};\\
 &\text{local Euler derivations}+\text{binary rigidity}
 \ \Longrightarrow\ \text{separated powers and unimodular Fermat};\\
 &\text{Pell rigidity}+\text{three- or four-square theorem}
 \ \Longrightarrow\ \text{quartic definitions of }\Z;\\
 &\text{real quadratic linear algebra}+\text{support-ring algebra}
 \ \Longrightarrow\ \text{quadratic-level dichotomy};\\
 &\text{normal forms}+\text{set-cut bounds}
 \ \Longrightarrow\ \text{common divisors and clearing};\\
 &\text{Pell rigidity and divisibility}+\text{intersective }\Lambda
 \ \Longrightarrow\ \text{order-free definition of }\Z,\ \Z[i];\\
 &\ct+\sqrt2\notin\Frac(\mathfrak o)
 \ \Longrightarrow\ \text{the Diophantine ideal}
 \ \Longrightarrow\ \text{graph of }\ct\text{ and homomorphisms};\\
 &\text{augmentation}+\text{root of }\Lambda\text{ in }\Bbbk
 \ \Longrightarrow\ \text{detector and ideal test};\\
 &\text{Diophantine ideal}+\text{one negative exponent}
 \ \Longrightarrow\ \text{multiplier identity};\\
 &\text{multiplier identity}+\text{real closedness of }\No
 \ \Longrightarrow\ \text{reconstruction of }\R\text{ and }\No ;\\
 &\text{retraction}+\text{B\'ezout identities}
 \ \Longrightarrow\ \text{multiplier theorem}
 \ \Longrightarrow\ \text{denominator dichotomy};\\
 &\text{matrix completion}+\text{set-cut bounds}
 \ \Longrightarrow\ \text{congruence orbits, focusing, dense fibers};\\
 &\text{Euler degrees}+\text{image condition}+\gcd(P,P')=1
 \ \Longrightarrow\ \text{squarefree rigidity};\\
 &\text{two opposite rings}+\text{properness}+\text{global forms}
 \ \Longrightarrow\ \text{proper rigidity};\\
 &\text{positive genus}+\text{units}+\text{forms of }\Aff^1
 \ \Longrightarrow\ \text{smooth affine curves};\\
 &\text{smooth affine curves}+\ct+\text{separatedness}
 \ \Longrightarrow\ \text{arithmetic fibers and descent};\\
 &\text{image condition}+\text{properness}+\text{logarithmic poles}
 \ \Longrightarrow\ \text{logarithmic tangent criterion};\\
 &\text{conductor}+\text{Seidenberg's identity}+\text{discrete boundary place}
 \ \Longrightarrow\ \text{singular affine curves};\\
 &\text{Euler family}+\text{velocity certificate}
 \ \Longrightarrow\ \text{discriminant translation}.
\end{align*}
The source-05 proofs that most deserve independent review are, by its own
account, the separated-power theorem (divisibility in a quotient ring without
unique factorization), the unimodular Fermat theorem (strict leading-term
cancellation in the equal-degree Wronskian), and the set-sized quotient
theorem (positivity of every exponent in the geometric quotient; printed in
the sibling report). Sources C10 and C13 ask for specialist review of the
scheme-valued two-ring argument at the level of its functorial maps, and of the exact
arithmetic fibers; the elementary chain of the squarefree theorem is independent of
algebraic geometry, and the Weierstrass result has both proofs. Source C15's proof audit
asks first for review of the functorial contraction and of the passage from logarithmic
annihilation to constancy, then of the set-sized formulation of the class applications. Source C16's audit lists fifteen critical checks; it singles out the commensurability of
the conductor and the contracted differential at the boundary place as the step that would
fail without discreteness (\cref{odg:sg:rem:discrete}).
Source C17's proof audit singles out the ideal intersection that kills the coefficients of
the velocity polynomial, the characteristic polynomial of a logarithmic derivative on a
finite projective module, and the passage from fixed eigenvalues to fixed eigenspaces by
positivity (\cref{odg:disc:prop:velocities,odg:disc:prop:unitD,odg:disc:lem:projection}).

\subsection{Finite checks}

Fifteen scripts are shipped in \fn{code/}, each with its recorded output in
\fn{data/}. The first three need Python~3.9 or later and SymPy (tested with
SymPy 1.14.0); source 06's needs Python~3.10 or later and SymPy, and source
07's Python~3.10 or later and the standard library only; source 08's needs
Python~3.10 or later and source 09's Python~3.9 or later, both with SymPy. Of
the scripts of sources C10--C14, source C11's needs Python~3.10 or later and the
standard library only, source C12's Python~3.9 or later with SymPy, and the others
Python~3.10 or later with SymPy; source C15's needs Python~3.9 or later and SymPy, and
source C16's Python~3.10 or later and SymPy, and source C17's Python~3.10 or later and
SymPy 1.14.0. They
check finite identities and examples only.

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*]
\item \fn{01-diophantine-geometry-verification.py}: scalar identities
(Pythagorean and hyperboloid identities and their B\'ezout certificates,
the cubic norm by resultant, the Euclid ratio), 20 quadratic-isometry cases in
dimensions 3 to 7, Pell indices 0 to 30, and 256 guard witnesses. It writes a
JSON report, by default \fn{verification_report.json} in the working
directory, so it should be run with \texttt{--output} pointing elsewhere or
on a copy; the shipped record is
\fn{01-diophantine-geometry-verification_report.json}.
\item \fn{02-diophantine-verify_examples.py}: 13 exact identity checks
(Pythagorean, cubic norm two ways, $N^3=0$, skewness, Lorentz preservation,
determinant, group law and inverse, the hyperboloid family, binomial
coefficients, the Euclid recurrence) and 49 finite-support products; output
only to the terminal, recorded in \fn{02-diophantine-verification.txt}.
\item \fn{05-diophantine-rigidity-verify_examples.py}: nine groups (Pell
recurrence, Pythagorean witness, the $\rho$ identity, 64 separated-power
identities, Fermat Wronskian identities for $2\le p\le10$, 16 finite
telescoping identities, binomial root coefficients, quartic-definition
certificates for $|t|\le40$ by default, and five points on $y^2=x^3+1$);
output to the terminal, recorded in
\fn{05-diophantine-rigidity-verification.txt}.
\item \fn{06-definability-reconstruction-verify.py}: the degrees of the
quintic (five, seven witnesses) and of source 06's degree-ten polynomial for
the graph of $\ct$, the displayed real witnesses, six Gaussian examples of the
three-equation system, the Pell unit order for moduli $1$ to $500$, roots of
$\Lambda$ modulo $1$ to $2000$, the four-square product identity, and
finite-support ideal and twist identities. It writes a JSON report only to
the path given with \texttt{--output} and otherwise prints it; the shipped
record is \fn{06-definability-reconstruction-verification.json}.
\item \fn{07-defining-arithmetic-verify.py}: 13 groups (the expansion of
$\Lambda$, the universal witness identity of \cref{odg:def:thm:augdetector}
symbolically, the witness degrees, the certificate for $1+\omega$, roots of
$\Lambda$ modulo $1$ to $4096$, the $2$-adic lifting step through $2^{33}$,
Pell identities to index $300$, the modular return for moduli $1$ to $500$,
witnesses of the system for $-6\le x\le6$ and six Gaussian samples, quartic
witnesses for $-100\le x\le100$, the degree of $F_4$ and its Gaussian
collapse); output to the terminal only, recorded in
\fn{07-defining-arithmetic-verification.txt}. It uses assertions and must
not be run with \texttt{python -O}.
\item \fn{08-fractions-verify.py}: 833 exact assertions in 13 categories,
among them 40 random polynomial vectors (seed 23092026) with polynomial and
integer B\'ezout identities, the lifted certificates \eqref{odg:frac:eq:lift},
the rational or irrational classification over $\Q(\sqrt2)$, sample scalar
memberships, the examples, the substitution $T=U^m$ and the defect layers, the
nonflat relation, integrality equations, the residue identities and
falling-factorial independence. It writes a JSON report, by default
\fn{verification.json} next to the script, so it should be run with
\texttt{--output} pointing elsewhere or on a copy; the shipped record is
\fn{08-fractions-verification.json}.
\item \fn{09-fraction-fibres-verify_examples.py}: 92 exact assertions (the
matrices $\mathsf F_{x,b}$: nilpotence, determinant, composition, inverse,
action, fixed centre and image of $\infty$; the covariance
\eqref{odg:frac:eq:covariance}; B\'ezout certificates such as
\eqref{odg:frac:eq:closebezout}; nine one-scale examples with coprimality,
specialization, direction type and lifted certificates; the local density
construction; five finite focusing samples over $\Q$ and the image of
$\infty$). It always writes \fn{verification_report.json} next to the
script, so it should be run on a copy; the shipped record is
\fn{09-fraction-fibres-verification_report.json}.
\item \fn{10-curve-rigidity-verify.py}: 3,991 exact assertions in eight groups (the
cubic certificate and its differential form, squarefree certificates for eleven
polynomials and $2\le m\le8$, the degree obstruction on the grid $2\le d,m\le30$, the
nodal family, binomial coefficients, the constant-term boundary, finite-support Euler
laws in lexicographic ranks one to three, and lexicographic clearing). It writes
\fn{verification.json} next to itself unless \texttt{--output} is given; the shipped
record is \fn{10-curve-rigidity-verification.json}, and
\fn{10-curve-rigidity-BUILD_AUDIT.json} records source C10's build of its own article.
\item \fn{11-curve-abelian-rigidity-verify.py}: 13,049 exact assertions in 19
categories over $\Q$ and $\mathbb F_2$ (the universal cubic certificate, the
divisibility identity, finite-support Euler laws, the characteristic-two tail and
Frobenius squares, the discretely ordered ring's parity and leading terms, binomial
coefficients, the dual-number example). It writes \fn{verification.json} next to itself
unless \texttt{--output} is given, and prints the report; the shipped record is
\fn{11-curve-abelian-rigidity-verification.json}.
\item \fn{12-curve-logarithmic-verification.py}: 49 recorded checks, among them 35
differential-ideal certificates for five squarefree polynomials and $2\le m\le8$, the
cubic certificate and discriminant, the singular parametrization, the
nonconstant-coefficient example, the integer threshold $(m-1)d\ge m$, the coefficients of
the elliptic root in \cref{odg:cr:ex:denominator}, and 400 finite-support Euler trials
over lexicographic $\Z^2$. It writes \fn{verification_report.json} next to itself unless
\texttt{--output} is given; the shipped record is
\fn{12-curve-logarithmic-verification_report.json}.
\item \fn{13-differential-rigidity-verify_certificates.py}: 12,874 exact assertions in
17 checks (the universal cubic certificate, the general Weierstrass completion,
squarefree and differential certificates, degree inequalities, finite Hahn Leibniz,
support, degree and detection tests over lexicographic pairs, the two-ring inclusions,
the single-derivation caution, sharpness and affine-line fiber identities). It prints
its report and writes a file only to the path given with \texttt{--output}; the shipped
record is \fn{13-differential-rigidity-verification_report.txt}.
\item \fn{14-hahn-differential-rigidity-verify.py}: 3,440 exact assertions in five
groups (symbolic certificates, finite series, degrees, arithmetic including the
congruence example, and characteristic-two telescoping). It always writes
\fn{verification.json} next to itself, so it should be run on a copy; the shipped
record is \fn{14-hahn-differential-rigidity-verification.json}, and
\fn{14-hahn-differential-rigidity-BUILD_AUDIT.json} records source C14's build of its
own article.
\item \fn{15-logarithmic-rigidity-verify.py}: 2,820 cases in seven groups (the universal
cubic identity and its case $\Delta_0=23$, 49 differentiated superelliptic certificates
for seven squarefree polynomials and $2\le m\le8$, 2,401 valuation-margin samples for
$2\le d,m\le50$, the cusp and node families, 300 finite rank-two Hahn examples checking
the Leibniz rule, supports, valuations and constant terms, 64 characteristic-two
telescoping checks with the three partial derivatives, and a truncated binomial series
for the valuation-ring point). It prints its report and writes a file only to the path
given with \texttt{--output}; the shipped record is
\fn{15-logarithmic-rigidity-verification.json}, and
\fn{15-logarithmic-rigidity-build_report.json} records source C15's build of its own
article.
\item \fn{16-singular-curves-verify.py}: 113,940 exact assertions in 28 groups: 264 rational
and 8 symbolic checks of Seidenberg's identity; 1,493 checks of finite-support Hahn
arithmetic over $\Q$ (Leibniz rule, constant terms, valuations, iterated supports and
leading-term detection); 200 lexicographic rank-two comparisons for the non-normality
example; the superelliptic test for all 36,071 multiplicity patterns with $2\le m\le20$ and
one to three nonzero residues (genus integrality, the equivalence of
\cref{odg:sg:thm:super}, invariance under full $m$th powers) and 21 normalization
identities; the elliptic B\'ezout and differential identities, the discriminant $-23$ and the
normalization of the pinched curve; the conductor memberships $xy^j\in\Bbbk[C]$ for $j<15$;
the node, cusp, acnode and real signed parametrizations; and 3,696 checks of the
derivative-order bound for $1\le\ell\le8$, $0\le\delta\le20$, $0\le r\le10$. It prints its
report and writes a file only to the path given with \texttt{--output}; the shipped record
is \fn{16-singular-curves-verification.json}, and \fn{16-singular-curves-build_report.json}
records source C16's build of its own article.
\item \fn{17-discriminant-rigidity-verify.py}: 1,126 exact assertions in 13 groups, with the
fixed seed 20260923: 96 translation checks (extracted shift, constant extraction, inverse
substitution, marked value) for random integer roots in degrees 1 to 6, 20 discriminants
and 20 translated factorizations; 420 checks of \eqref{odg:disc:eq:wformula} and of the
clearing by $\Delta^2$, 510 of homogeneity, edge-permutation invariance and the velocity
characteristic polynomial, and 6 of \eqref{odg:disc:eq:sumvelocity}, in degrees 2 to 6; 2
quadratic-certificate checks; 8 resultant and 18 critical-configuration checks; 9 matrix
checks; 8 characteristic-$p$ checks for $p=2,3,5$; 7 counterexample checks; and the
discriminants $5$ and $4$ of \cref{odg:disc:ex:infinite,odg:disc:ex:critical}. It writes
its report to the path given with \texttt{--output}, by default \fn{verification.json} in
the current directory, and prints it; the shipped record is
\fn{17-discriminant-rigidity-verification.json}, and
\fn{17-discriminant-rigidity-BUILD_REPORT.json} records source C17's build of its own
article.
\end{itemize}
When this report was assembled the first three passed with Python 3.14.4 and
SymPy 1.14.0, and source 05's script also passed with

the quintic over $\Og$ in \cref{odg:def:rem:quintic} were checked separately
when this report was assembled; the shipped scripts are unchanged.
The scripts of sources 08 and 09 passed with Python 3.14.4 and SymPy 1.14.0,
on copies, during placement and again when \cref{odg:frac:sec:fractions} was
written. Source 08's report (833 assertions) has the same content as its

\cref{odg:frac:rem:elementary,odg:frac:rem:lift} and the table of

was written; the shipped scripts are unchanged.
The five scripts of sources C10--C14 passed with Python 3.14.4 and SymPy 1.14.0 when

checks. Their reports differ from the shipped records only in the recorded Python
version (3.13.5 there) and, for source C10, the run time and timestamp; source C11's
record contains neither and its report is identical. The identities printed in

parametrizations and identities printed in
\cref{odg:sg:sub:super,odg:sg:sub:example,odg:sg:cor:hyper} were checked separately with

\section{Repository and literature audit}\label{odg:app:repo}\label{odg:app:audit}

\subsection{Repository snapshots}

Sources 01, 02 and 05 pin \pin{} (``Merge origin/main after the inverse-sine
series formalization''). Source 01 read the tree, the root README, the
documentation index, the trigonometry listing and its period-arithmetic
source audit; source 02 read the README, the documentation index and the
opening of the normal-form bridge note; source 05 read the documentation
index and the bridge note. Each source says that this was a focused
inspection through the GitHub connector, not a checkout, build, or review of
every manuscript and declaration. Sources 06 and 07 pin \texttt{71e9606}.
Source 06 read the root README, the documentation index, the opening of
\fn{docs/FORMALIZATION.md} and the first 235 source lines of the surcomplex
automorphism report; source 07 read the root README, the documentation index
and the opening of \fn{docs/FORMALIZATION.md}. Both call this a focused
comparison, not an exhaustive audit, and neither modified or built the
repository. Sources 08 and 09 pin \texttt{9a385d3}. Source 08 read the root
README, the tree, the documentation catalogue, the guides of this report and
of the sibling report, and source 06's source audit; source 09 read this
report's article and README, the root README and the collection guide. Both
call this a selective inspection through the connector, without a checkout or
build. Sources C10, C13 and C14 pin \texttt{4cdeaec} and read the report's
article at blob \texttt{4c26f4e}: source C10 its lines 1--130, 1350--1690 and
2910--3100, the README and the catalogues; source C13 lines 1--230, 1000--1200,
1460--1670, 2650--2860 and 2980--3125, the README and the opening of
\fn{docs/FORMALIZATION.md}; source C14 lines 1--180, 1000--1185, 1550--1785 and
2920--3080, the README, the catalogue and other reports' READMEs. Source C11 pins
\texttt{f6e031a} and compared against the report's README and the catalogue. Source
C12 pins \texttt{89bec38} and read Sections 6--7 of the article (blob
\texttt{47af413}), its README, the root README and the catalogue. All five call this a
targeted inspection through the connector, without a checkout or build. Source C15 pins
\texttt{934810a} and read, at that pin, the first 180 lines of
\fn{docs/FORMALIZATION.md}, lines 130--280 of this report's article (blob
\texttt{cfc25a5}), lines 1--150 and 360--540 of its README and the first 210 lines of the
sibling report's article, through the connector and without a build. Source C16 pins
\texttt{bcac55a} and read, through the connector, the repository metadata and recursive tree,
the root and documentation READMEs, the directory and README of this report, and the audits
of sources C13 and C14 in full; it did not read the assembled article in full and did not
build the repository. Source C17 pins \texttt{efc5446} (tree \texttt{aba6982}) and read
the root README, the documentation catalogue, the guides of this report, of the sibling
report, of the omnific automorphism report and of the independent-copies report, and the
opening of the holonomic-rigidity article, some through excerpts or a truncated catalogue
response; it did not read this report's article and did not build the repository. The checks of
all these repository statements against the placement commits are in
\cref{odg:app:provenance}.

\subsection{Literature checks}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*]
\item L'Innocente--Mantova, arXiv:1710.07304v5: Theorem~B states that
$\omega^{\sqrt2}+\omega+1$ is prime in $\Oz$, answering a question of Gonshor;
their Section~1.3 reviews the earlier results on $1+\sum_k\omega^{1/k}$; their
introduction records, citing Shepherdson, that the nonnegative part of $\Oz$
is a model of open induction. The citations of all three sources agree with
this.
\item Shiu (\emph{Math.\ Gazette} 104 (2020)) and the Archive of Formal Proofs
entry of Danilkin and Chevalier (2023) for the three-square theorem, and
Ehrlich--Kaplan Lemma 11.1 (as also cited in the trigonometry report), were
confirmed during placement.
\item Je\v r\'abek's MLQ article on rigid models of Presburger arithmetic
(source 01) is cited for background; it was not re-read for this report.
\item Source 02's MathOverflow attribution could not be checked
(\cref{odg:ex:fermatscaling}).
\item Matiyasevich's page range is corrected as described in
\cref{odg:app:provenance}.
\item Source 06's attribution to \emph{nombre} was confirmed during placement
through the Stack Exchange interface: a comment of 6 July 2018 on
MathOverflow question 304290 defines the ordinary integers in $\Oz$ by
excluding nonzero solutions of $x^2=2y^2$ between $-|n|$ and $|n|$, and
observes that $\Oz$ is not saturated \cite{Nombre}.
\item Je\v r\'abek's answer of 15 August 2011 to MathOverflow question 72691,
cited by sources 06 and 07, was confirmed: it notes that the theory of $\Oz$ is
a complete extension of Robinson's arithmetic and hence not recursively
axiomatizable \cite{JerabekMO}.
\item L\^e--Spencer, arXiv:1309.2259v1, Section 2, Example 4 lists $\Lambda$ and
attributes it to Borevich--Shafarevich, page 3; Berend--Bilu is
\emph{Proc.\ Amer.\ Math.\ Soc.}\ 124 (1996), 1663--1671. Miller--Shlapentokh,
arXiv:1706.03302, cited by source 07 as related Pell-method precedent for
function rings, has the stated title \cite{MillerShlapentokh}. These were
confirmed during placement; Borevich--Shafarevich itself was not re-read.
\item The following were confirmed during placement for sources 08 and 09. In
the arXiv version 1710.07304v5 of L'Innocente--Mantova, Proposition 2.4.5
states a
cofinality criterion for the fraction field of $\Z+K((G^{<0}))$ to be
$K((G))$, and records $\Frac(\Oz)=\No$ as Conway's Theorem 32; their Lemma
9.2.1 is a primality
transfer along an algebra retraction, and ``reduced'' is a support notion in
their paper. Hamkins's post of 4 November 2025 \cite{Hamkins} says in its
public part that lowest terms fail and that $\sqrt2$ and $\pi$ are omnific
fractions; it does not discuss gcds there. Klawa's arXiv:2211.13412v2
\cite{Klawa} exists with the stated title and is cited by source 09 for
background only. Stacks Tag 00HK is the equational criterion of flatness
\cite{StacksFlat}.
\item For sources C10--C14 the following were checked during placement. Stacks Tag
0BX5 is Lemma 29.43.1, the valuative criterion of properness for arbitrary valuation
rings, inside the section Tag 0BX4 cited by sources C10 and C11; Tag 0BS6 is Lemma
53.5.2 (Riemann--Roch) inside Section 53.5, Tag 0B5B. Tag 02AT, cited by source C13
for differentials, is Section 111.50 of the chapter of exercises, so Tag 00RM,
cited by sources C10 and C14, is used instead (\cref{odg:app:provenance}). The other
Stacks tags and the statement numbers in Milne's \emph{Abelian Varieties} and
\emph{Algebraic Groups} are cited as the sources give them and were not rechecked for
this report.
\item Van den Dries's paper \cite{vdDries} is cited by sources C11 and C14 from its
bibliographic record and the abstract reproduced in the author's bibliography on
Celebratio Mathematica, where the abstract says that a criterion is given for curves
over $\Z$ ``to have an infinite point in a discrete ordered ring''. The placement check
confirmed that abstract; neither source nor this report read the paper itself, no
result here depends on it, and no comparison or priority statement is made
(\cref{odg:cr:sub:dor}). Source C15 cites it in the same way, from its record and
abstract, under the publisher's DOI 10.1090/S0002-9947-1981-0597875-5.
\item For source C15 the following were checked during placement: Stacks Tag 0FMU is
Section 50.15, \emph{Log poles along a divisor}; Tag 0BY6 is Section 53.8, the genus of a
curve; Tag 0BYA is Section 53.9, plane curves, with the genus $(d-1)(d-2)/2$; and Tag
0A22 is Section 33.43, curves, with the remark on compactification. Its citations of
Milne's \emph{Abelian Varieties} (Chapter III, Proposition 2.3; Chapter IV, Proposition
6.4) were not rechecked.
\item For source C16 the following were checked when \cref{odg:sg:sub:singular} was
written. Seidenberg's \emph{Derivations and integral closure} is Pacific J.\ Math.\ 16 (1966),
167--173 \cite{Seidenberg}. Ciuperc\u{a}'s \emph{Integral closure of strongly Golod ideals} is
Nagoya Math.\ J.\ 241, 204--216, first published online on 18 July 2019 \cite{Ciuperca}.
Stacks Tag 035E is Section 29.55, \emph{Normalization}, and Tag 0C1B is Section 53.12,
\emph{Riemann--Hurwitz}, whose Lemma 53.12.3 says that the differential of a uniformizer
freely generates the module of differentials at a closed point with separable residue
field. Its other Stacks tags (0BXX, 0BY3, 0BX5, 0BS6, 0C6L) are cited above. Seidenberg's
Section 3 and Ciuperc\u{a}'s Section 2.5 were not re-read; the identity is reproved in
\cref{odg:sg:lem:seidenberg}. Source C16 cites van den Dries from its record and abstract, as
sources C11, C14 and C15 do.
\item For source C17 the following were checked when \cref{odg:disc:sub:disc} was written.
Stacks Tag 00U0 is Section 10.143, \emph{\'Etale ring maps}; Tag 00UP is Section 10.150,
\emph{Formally \'etale maps}, containing Definition 10.150.1 and Lemma 10.150.3; Tag 0BJF is
Lemma 49.3.1, in Section 49.3, \emph{Discriminant of a finite locally free morphism}. The
Mathematics Stack Exchange question 1626152 of the user Oblomov (25 January 2016) could not
be retrieved from this environment and is cited as source C17 gives it
\cite{Oblomov}; nothing here depends on it. Its citation of L'Innocente--Mantova, Fact 2.1.1,
for the algebraic closedness of Hahn fields was not rechecked.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{A dated factorization announcement}

On September 18, 2026 Dan Abramov announced a Lean proof of Conway's
refinement conjecture \cite{Abramov,AbramovRepo}, noting explicitly the
absence of independent mathematical verification. None of the sources audited
that code or used the announced result. Accordingly this report does not
repeat older descriptions of the refinement conjecture as a verified
\emph{currently open} problem, does not claim to settle it, and none of its
proofs depends on its status.
